`market-service` gains a new `internal/circulation` package modelling the time dimension of capital's circuit: the period during which capital sits in money-form or commodity-form without producing surplus-value.

- **Domain types.** `TurnoverTime{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Production, Circulation, CirculationOpen}` with `.TotalNanos()` and `.ActiveFractionBasisPoints()` (10000 × production / total). `ProductionTime{LabourTimeNanos, LabourInterruptionNanos, LatentNanos, NaturalProcessNanos}`. `CirculationTime{SellingTimeNanos, BuyingTimeNanos}`. `SellingPhase{…, Outcome SellingOutcome}` (sold/spoiled/partial/pending). `BuyingPhase{…, MarketLocation}`. `NaturalProcessSpan{Process NaturalProcessKind, DurationNanos}` (ripening/fermentation/tanning/drying/other). `LatentProductiveCapital{Pence, HeldAt, EnteredProductionAt *time.Time}`. `Perishability{CommodityID, WindowNanos}` with `.Spoiled(elapsed) bool`. `MarketSeparation{SellingMarketID, BuyingMarketID}`. `CirculationCompressionTarget{TargetCirculationNanos}`.
- **Sentinel errors.** `ErrConcurrentProductionAndCirculation`, `ErrSellingPhaseAlreadyOpen`, `ErrBuyingPhaseAlreadyOpen`, `ErrNoOpenSellingPhase`, `ErrNoOpenBuyingPhase`.
- **Store.** `TurnoverTimeStore` interface embedded in `Store`. Memory and MySQL implementations. Migrations `00003_v2_ch05_circulation_time.sql` and `00004_v2_ch05_seed.sql`: SpinningMill1871 (1-week production + 1-week circulation), grain/wine/leather natural-process spans, milk (2d) and beer (7d) perishability rows, £50 cotton stockpile latent capital.
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/turnover-time` (201), `GET /v1/turnover-time` (200 list), `GET /v1/turnover-time/{id}` (200/404), `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/labour-time` (200), `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/labour-interruption` (200), `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/latent-mp` (201), `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/natural-process` (201), `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/selling-phase` (open: 201, close: 200), `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/buying-phase` (open: 201, close: 200), `GET /v1/turnover-time/{id}/active-fraction` (200), `POST /v1/perishability` (201), `POST /v1/market-separation` (201).
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 5 — The Time of Circulation" panel: (1) list of turnover records, (2) stacked-bar of production sub-spans + circulation sub-spans with active-fraction badge, (3) zero-circulation toggle showing rise in active fraction toward 100%, (4) natural-process examples (grain 90d / wine 14d / leather 30d), (5) perishability cliff widget counting elapsed time against commodity spoilage windows.

### Vol. II Ch. 6 — what was built

`market-service` gains a new `internal/costs` package modelling the costs that capital bears during its circulation phase — the deductions from surplus-value that arise not from production but from the metamorphoses of commodity-form and money-form.

- **Domain types.** `CirculationCost{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Kind, Nature, Pence, IncurredAt}` — faux frais classified as `value_preserving` or `value_adding`. `CirculationCostKind` enum: `purchase_sale_time`, `book_keeping`, `money_as_faux_frais`, `supply_formation`, `commodity_supply`, `transportation`. `NatureOf(kind)` returns `value_adding` only for transportation. `CirculationAgent{…, LabourMinutesNecessary, LabourMinutesSurplus}` with `.ValueContribution() == 0` (circulation labour adds no value) and `.WageEffectivePence()`. `MoneyAsFauxFrais{Pence, AnnualReplacementPence}` — §I(c) national gold reserve wearing down as coin. `CommoditySupply{IsVoluntary bool, StorageCosts []StorageCost}` — voluntary reserve vs involuntary glut. `StorageCost{BuildingPence, LabourPence, PreservationLabourPence}` with `.Total()`. `TransportTariff{BasePencePerTonMile, FragilityMultiplierBasisPoints, BreakageRiskMultiplierBasisPoints}` with `.EffectiveRatePencePerTonMile()` applying the max multiplier in basis points. `TransportLeg{LabourCostPence, MeansOfTransportPence, SurplusPence}` with `.AddedValue()` — the value transport adds to the commodity.
- **Store.** `CirculationCostStore` interface embedded in `Store`. Memory and MySQL implementations (`mysql_costs.go`). Migrations `00005_v2_ch06_circulation_costs.sql` (7 tables) and `00006_v2_ch06_seed.sql`: Spinning Mill 1871 seller-agent, book-keeping cost 1200p, national gold reserve 1M/10K pence, voluntary + involuntary commodity supply with storage costs, Birmingham glass transport tariffs (historic 1×, modern 3× breakage-risk), two transport legs Birmingham→London.
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/circulation-costs` (201), `GET /v1/circulation-costs` (200 list with filters), `GET /v1/circulation-costs/{id}` (200/404), `GET /v1/circulation-costs/aggregate` (200), `GET /v1/circulation-costs/system-faux-frais` (200), `POST /v1/circulation-agents` (201), `POST /v1/money-as-faux-frais` (201), `POST /v1/commodity-supplies` (201 + Location), `POST /v1/commodity-supplies/{id}/storage-cost` (201/404), `POST /v1/transport-tariffs` (201, upsert by commodity), `POST /v1/transport-legs` (201 + Location).
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 6 — The Costs of Circulation" panel: (1) circulation costs list with nature pills (faux frais / value-adding), (2) 3-card aggregate (value-preserving / value-adding / total), (3) system faux-frais money-reserve block, (4) transport tariff cards showing effective rate with multiplier, (5) transport leg cards showing route, weight, distance, and added value.

### Vol. II Ch. 7 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/turnover/turnover.go` — Marx's analysis of the *turnover time* (u) and the *number of turnovers* (n = U/u, where U = 525,600 min = 1 year). Turnover is always measured on the productive or money circuit form; the commodity-capital form (Form III) is explicitly rejected for this analysis.

- **Domain types.** `Turnover{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Lens, TurnoverTimeMinutes, Cycles}`. `TurnoverCycle{ID, TurnoverID, StartedAt, EndedAt, AdvancePence, ReturnedPence, ProductionMinutes, CirculationMinutes}` with `.Validate()` (invariant: `returned >= advance`). `TurnoverNumber{ID, TurnoverID, YearReferenceMinutes, TurnoverTimeMinutes, BasisPoints}` where `BasisPoints = 10000 × YearReferenceMinutes / TurnoverTimeMinutes` (integer, no float64). `CircuitLens` (`"money"` | `"productive"`) — `"commodity"` is rejected with `ErrLensNotApplicableForTurnover`.
- **Sentinel errors.** `ErrLensNotApplicableForTurnover`, `ErrCycleNotComplete`, `ErrNoCompletedCycles`.
- **Pure functions.** `ValidateLens(l)`, `ComputeTurnoverNumber(turnoverID, avgMinutes)`, `AverageTurnoverTime(cycles)`.
- **Store.** `TurnoverStore` interface with `CreateTurnover`, `GetTurnover`, `ListTurnovers`, `RecordCycle`, `RecomputeNumber`, `GetNumber`. Memory and MySQL implementations. `RecomputeNumber` averages `production_minutes + circulation_minutes` across all recorded cycles and upserts `turnover_numbers`.
- **Migrations.** `00035_v2_ch07_turnover.sql` (tables: `turnovers`, `turnover_cycles`, `turnover_numbers` with `UNIQUE KEY uq_tn_turnover`). `00036_v2_ch07_seed.sql`: SpinningMill (productive, 10,080 min, BP=521,428 ≈ 52/year), LocomotiveMaker (money, 777,600 min, BP=6,759 ≈ 2/3/year), WineMaturer (productive, 2,102,400 min, BP=2,500 = 1/4/year).
- **HTTP.** `POST /v1/turnovers`, `GET /v1/turnovers`, `GET /v1/turnovers/{id}`, `POST /v1/turnovers/{id}/cycles`, `POST /v1/turnovers/{id}/recompute-number`, `GET /v1/turnovers/{id}/number`. Gateway proxies `/v1/turnovers` and `/v1/turnovers/{rest...}` to simulation-engine.
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 7 — The Turnover Time" panel: one card per turnover showing lens, turnover time (formatted as weeks/years), cycle count, n expressed as `BasisPoints / 10000` per year.
### Vol. II Ch. 8 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/composition/` — a new domain package modelling the fixed/circulating distinction within productive capital. The distinction applies only to productive capital; money-capital and commodity-capital are never divided this way (Marx, §I).

- **Domain types.** `CapitalComponent{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Kind, Role, Pence, EnteredAt}` — a single line-item of productive capital. `CapitalKind` enum: `fixed` | `circulating`. `RoleInProcess` enum: `instrument_of_labour` | `raw_material` | `auxiliary_material` | `labour_power`. `KindForRole(r)` — the invariant map: only instruments of labour are fixed; all others circulate (Marx rejects Ramsay's misclassification of auxiliary materials as fixed). `FixedCapitalItem{ID, CapitalComponentID, Description, PencePurchased, ServiceLifeMinutes, WearModel, PurchasedAt, RetiredAt}` with `Validate()` (service_life_minutes > 0). `WearAndTear{ID, FixedCapitalItemID, Pence, PeriodCoveredMinutes, CalculatedAt, Cause}`. `WearCause` enum: `use` | `atmospheric` | `moral_depreciation`. `WearModel` enum: `linear` | `quadratic`. `ComputeLinearWear(item, periodMinutes, cause)` — integer formula `pence = pence_purchased × period / service_life`; sums to `PencePurchased` over the item's lifetime. `SinkingFundForItem{AccumulatedPence, TargetPence}` — auto-created on `RegisterFixedItem`; accumulates via `RecordWear`. `Repair{Kind, Pence, ServiceLifeExtensionMinutes}` — `RepairRunning` must not extend life; `RepairLarge` must extend life. `FixedCapitalSubcomponent` — homogeneous constituent with its own wear rate (railway rails vs sleepers vs earthworks). `CirculatingCycle` — one full replacement cycle of a circulating-capital component. `SinkingFundReinvestment{Kind: intensive|extensive}` — reproduction on extended scale from released fixed-capital value.
- **Store.** `CompositionStore` interface in `store.go`. `memoryComposition` in `memory_composition.go` (in-memory, for tests). `mysql_composition.go` (production). Migrations `00037_v2_ch08_composition.sql` (8 tables) and `00038_v2_ch08_seed.sql`: £10,000 10-year spinning machine (Marx §I), British railway with unequal subcomponents (rails 2yr, sleepers 8yr, earthworks 80yr), auxiliary material (lubricating oil) and raw material (iron ore).
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/capital-components` (201), `GET /v1/capital-components` (200, filters: industrial_capital_id/kind/role), `GET /v1/capital-components/{id}` (200/404), `POST /v1/fixed-items` (201 + auto-creates sinking fund), `GET /v1/fixed-items/{id}` (200/404), `POST /v1/fixed-items/{id}/subcomponents` (201), `POST /v1/fixed-items/{id}/wear` (201, accumulates into sinking fund), `GET /v1/fixed-items/{id}/sinking-fund` (200/404), `POST /v1/fixed-items/{id}/repairs` (201, large repairs extend service life), `POST /v1/fixed-items/{id}/reinvestments` (201), `POST /v1/circulating-cycles` (201).
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 8 — Fixed Capital and Circulating Capital" panel: fixed capital grid (component cards + fixed-item cards with sinking-fund progress bar) and circulating capital grid. Seed data from the spinning-machine and railway fixtures visible on first load.

### Vol. II Ch. 4 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/circulation/industrial_capital.go` to synthesise the three circuit-forms into a single object: `IndustrialCapital`. Where Chs. 1–3 each presented one uninterrupted circuit form in isolation, Ch. 4 shows that every real industrial capital is permanently divided across all three stages simultaneously — Marx's *Nebeneinander* ("co-existence"). A stoppage at any one stage ripples as disorder into the co-existence of the other two.

- **Domain types.** `IndustrialCapital{ID, AgentID, MoneyCircuitID, ProductiveCircuitID, CommodityCircuitID, TotalPence, EconomyMode, StagnationToleranceTicks, Status, LatestDistribution, OpenBlocks, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt}`. `CapitalPart{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Pence, Stage, EnteredStageAt}`. `StageDistribution{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, At, MoneyPence, ProductionPence, CommodityPence}` with `.Total()` and `.Validate(expectedTotal)`. `StageBlock{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Stage, Reason, OpenedAt, ClosedAt}` with `.IsOpen()`. `ValueRevolutionEvent{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Affecting, DirectionPence, OccurredAt}`. `MoneyCapitalSetFree` / `MoneyCapitalTiedUp` records emitted by `ApplyValueRevolution`. `MetamorphosisInterlock{ID, BuyerIndustrialCapitalID, SellerIndustrialCapitalID, SellerMPSourceOrigin, Pence, OccurredAt}`. `SupplyDemandImbalance{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, Period, DemandPence, SupplyPence, ExcessPence}` computed by `ComputeSupplyDemandImbalance(oc, surplusPence)`. `AggregateSupplyDemandImbalance` — class-level totals. `SinkingFund{ID, IndustrialCapitalID, FixedCapitalPence, LifetimeYears, AnnualPaymentPence, AccumulatedPence}` with `.Validate()` (invariant: `annualPayment * lifetime == fixedCapital`) and `.Tick()`.
- **Sentinel errors.** `ErrNonCapitalCounterparty`, `ErrCreditWithoutMoneyEconomy`, `ErrSinkingFundMismatch`, `ErrNonZeroDirectionPence`.
- **Pure functions.** `ApplyValueRevolution(evt)` — routes the event to `MoneyCapitalSetFree` (prices fell) or `MoneyCapitalTiedUp` (prices rose). `ComputeSupplyDemandImbalance(ic, period, oc, surplusPence)` — demand = c+v, supply = c+v+s, excess = s. `OrganicComposition.RatioBasisPoints()` — 10000 × c / v (integer, no float64).
- **Store.** `IndustrialCapitalStore` interface with `CreateIndustrialCapital`, `GetIndustrialCapital`, `ListIndustrialCapitals`, `RecordCapitalPart`, `SnapshotStageDistribution`, `OpenStageBlock`, `CloseStageBlock`, `RecordValueRevolution`, `RecordInterlock`, `RecordSupplyDemand`, `GetSupplyDemand`, `AggregateSupplyDemand`, `SetSinkingFund`, `TickSinkingFund`. Memory and MySQL implementations. Migrations `00033_v2_ch04_industrial_capital.sql` (eight tables) and `00034_v2_ch04_seed.sql`: SpinningMill1871 seed capital (id `5eed000000000000000401`) with an opening stage distribution, a supply-demand imbalance, and a sinking fund.
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/industrial-capitals` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/industrial-capitals` (200 list), `GET /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}` (200/404, includes latest distribution + open blocks), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/parts` (201), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/snapshot` (201), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/blocks` (201), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/blocks/{block_id}/close` (200), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/value-revolution` (200), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/interlocks` (201), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/supply-demand` (201), `GET /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/supply-demand` (200), `GET /v1/industrial-capitals/supply-demand/aggregate` (200), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/sinking-fund` (201), `POST /v1/industrial-capitals/{id}/sinking-fund/tick` (200), `GET /v1/historical-stages/{id}/genesis` (200). api-gateway proxies `/v1/industrial-capitals`, `/v1/industrial-capitals/{rest...}`, and `/v1/historical-stages/{rest...}` to simulation-engine.
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 4 — The Three Formulas of the Circuit" panel: the three circuit formulas (I, II, III) displayed as monospace code blocks, a card list of industrial capitals with colour-coded stage status (M=blue, P=green, C′=orange, blocked=red), a proportional M/P/C′ stage-distribution bar, distribution detail (pence per stage), and open stagnation-block badges.

### Volume III Roadmap — The Process of Capitalist Production as a Whole

Spec sweep pending; titles below are drawn from the vault filenames at `marx-engels/1894/capital-volume-iii/texts/` and will be refined as each chapter spec is authored. Primary-service column is a planning guess; most rows are `finance-service` with `simulation-engine` for cross-capital aggregations (average rate of profit, formation of prices of production).

| Chapter   | Status      | Concepts                                                                                          | Primary services                  |
|-----------|-------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| Ch. 1     | ✅ Done     | Cost-Price and Profit (k + p) — surplus-value reappears as profit on total capital                | finance-service                   |

| Ch. 3     | ✅ Done     | Relation of the Rate of Profit to the Rate of Surplus-Value — p′ = s′(v/C); variation of s′ and v/C | finance-service                   |
| Ch. 4     | ✅ Done     | The Effect of the Turnover on the Rate of Profit — annual rate of profit p′ = s′·n·(v/C)            | finance-service                   |
| Ch. 5     | ✅ Done     | Economy in the Employment of Constant Capital — a saving in c raises p′ = s/(c+v), leaving s and v untouched | finance-service                   |
| Ch. 6     | ✅ Done     | The Effect of Price Fluctuation on the Rate of Profit — a re-pricing of raw-material c moves p′ = s/(c+v); release/tie-up of capital in stock | finance-service                   |
| Ch. 7     | ✅ Done     | Supplementary Remarks (on the rate of profit) — organic-composition channel; refutation of Rodbertus; Part I summary | finance-service                   |
| Ch. 8     | ✅ Done     | Different Compositions of Capitals in Different Branches → differences in rates of profit          | finance-service                   |
| Ch. 9     | ✅ Done     | Formation of a General Rate of Profit; capital-weighted general rate p̄′=ΣS/ΣC; average profit; prices of production; value conservation | finance-service|

| Ch. 13    | ✅ Done     | The Law of the Tendential Fall in the Rate of Profit — As Such; rising organic composition with constant s′ ⇒ falling p′=s/(c+v); the rate-vs-mass contradiction; absolute over-accumulation | finance-service |
| Ch. 14    | ✅ Done     | Counteracting Influences — the six counter-tendencies (intensified exploitation, depressed wages, cheaper constant capital, relative over-population, foreign trade, stock capital); they retard the fall without abolishing it | finance-service |
| Ch. 15    | ✅ Done     | Exposition of the Internal Contradictions of the Law — §I falling rate / rising mass coexist; §II over-production; §III over-accumulation / relative over-population; §IV concentration & centralisation; crisis writes down c and restores p′ (**completes Part III**) | finance-service                   |

### Vol. II Ch. 9 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/turnover/aggregate_turnover.go` — the `AggregateTurnover` type that applies `AggregateTurnoverFormula` across fixed and circulating contributions to produce the mean-average turnover number in basis-points. `ComponentTurnoverContribution` stores each component's individual turnover number and annual-return pence alongside an enum `DifferenceKind` (more_than_one_year | less_than_one_year | exactly_one_year). `LifetimeCycle` records multi-year industrial cycles; `CrisisCyclePhaseRecord` annotates crisis phases (depression | medium_activity | precipitancy | crisis). Sentinels: `ErrNoContributions`, `ErrMissingIndustrialCapital`. Store: `AggregateTurnoverStore` interface, `memory_aggregate_turnover.go`, `mysql_aggregate_turnover.go`. Migrations `00039_v2_ch09_aggregate_turnover.sql` and `00040_v2_ch09_seed.sql` (SpinningMill1871 aggregate-turnover seed). Endpoints: `POST /v1/aggregate-turnovers`, `GET /v1/aggregate-turnovers`, `GET /v1/aggregate-turnovers/{id}`, `POST /v1/aggregate-turnovers/{id}/recompute`, `POST /v1/aggregate-turnovers/{id}/lifetime-cycle`, `POST /v1/aggregate-turnovers/{id}/crisis-phase`. React panel: contribution table, lifetime-cycle list, crisis-phase badges.

### Vol. II Ch. 10 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/circulation/economist_attribution.go` — documentation-only types for pre-Marxian political economy. `KnownEconomistError` enum (three values): `error_smith_conflation` (fixed/circulating ≠ constant/variable), `error_smith_circulation_capital_conflation` (money+commodity capital ≠ "circulating capital"), `error_smith_revenue_in_capital` (revenue flows ≠ capital). `EconomistAttribution{ID, Concept, Theorist, EditionYear, Anticipates, Errors}` — static reference data, no domain behaviour. `AllKnownEconomistErrors` slice for build-time coverage checks. Store: `EconomistAttributionStore` interface (`ListEconomistAttributions(ctx, theorist string)`); memory store pre-seeded; MySQL reads from `economist_attributions` + `economist_attribution_errors` tables. Migrations `00041_v2_ch10_economist_attributions.sql` (DDL) and `00042_v2_ch10_seed.sql` (Quesnay 1758 × 2 rows + Smith 1776 × 1 row with all three error entries). Endpoint: `GET /v1/circulation/economist-attributions?theorist=`. React panel: two-column anticipations view (Quesnay's avances primitives/annuelles → Marx's fixed/circulating), "Where Smith Goes Wrong" accordion (three error entries with refutations), verbatim chapter quotes.

### Vol. II Ch. 11 — what was built

`simulation-engine` extends `internal/circulation/economist_attribution.go` — five new `KnownEconomistError` constants: `error_ricardo_durability_collapse` (Ricardo reduces fixed/circulating to physical durability; refuted by Ch. 8 KindForRole), `error_ricardo_conflation` (Ricardo's fixed ↔ constant / circulating ↔ variable conflation; refuted by Ch. 8), `error_ricardo_fixed_capital_price_explanation` (Ricardo observes organic-composition price effects but mis-locates the cause; deferred to Vol. III), `error_ricardo_no_aggregate_turnover` (Ricardo lacks Ch. 9 aggregate-turnover formula), `error_ricardo_no_value_revolution` (Ricardo treats value as static; refuted by Ch. 4 ValueRevolutionEvent). `AllKnownEconomistErrors` grows from 3 to 8 entries. Migration `00043_v2_ch11_seed.sql`: one `economist_attributions` row for Ricardo (1817, concept "fixed and circulating capital", anticipates "prices_of_production") + five `economist_attribution_errors` rows (seed ID `5eed000000000000001101`). No schema migration, no new Go store methods, no new HTTP endpoint — existing `GET /v1/circulation/economist-attributions?theorist=Ricardo` serves the data. React panel `Ch11TheoriesRicardo.tsx`: two-column durability table (Ricardo's four categories) beside Ch. 8 role-derives-kind map; "Where Ricardo Goes Wrong" error list (five entries with refutations); Vol. III deferred placeholder for price-of-production resolution.

### Vol. II Ch. 12 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/workperiod/working_period.go` — `WorkingPeriod` (discrete/connected modes; `Multiplier()` = 1 for discrete, `WorkingDayCount` for connected). `WorkingPeriodInterruption` (`DeteriorationPence == 0` enforced for discrete mode). `WorkingPeriodShortening` (4 kinds: cooperation / machinery / parallel_labour / bakewell_breeding; machinery requires `AdditionalFixedCapitalPence > 0`; parallel_labour requires `AdditionalLabourCount > 0`). `NaturalWorkingPeriodConstraint` enforces minimum via `ErrPrematureDelivery`. `WorkingPeriodCreditFinancing` (own + borrowed pence; `MortgageProviderID` placeholder for Vol. III Part Five). Store: `WorkingPeriodStore` interface, `memory_working_period.go`, `mysql_working_period.go`. Migrations `00044_v2_ch12_working_periods.sql` (DDL — 5 tables) and `00045_v2_ch12_seed.sql` (SpinningMill1871 7-day discrete; LocomotiveWorks 84-day connected; 5 natural constraints; interruption pair; 3 shortening rows; London-builder credit-financing; seed IDs `5eed00000000000000120*`). Endpoints: `POST/GET /v1/working-periods`, `GET /v1/working-periods/{id}`, `POST /v1/working-periods/{id}/interruptions`, `POST /v1/working-periods/{id}/shortenings`, `POST /v1/working-periods/{id}/credit-financing`, `POST/GET /v1/natural-constraints`. React panel `Ch12WorkingPeriod.tsx`: SpinningMill (1× multiplier) vs LocomotiveWorks (84× multiplier) side-by-side; natural-constraints reference table; shortenings list; Vol. III deferred placeholder for interest on borrowed capital.

### Vol. II Ch. 13 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds `internal/workperiod/production_time.go` — Ch. 13 deepens Ch. 12 by separating the production-time span into its two components: `ProductionLabourGap` records the split between total production-time and the labour-time contained within it for a given working period (`GapNanos = ProductionTimeNanos − LabourTimeNanos`); `NaturalProcessActivation` records a named natural-process span (10 kinds: ripening, fermentation, tanning, drying, other from Ch. 5; plus agricultural_growth, forest_growth, animal_maturation, chemical, seasonal_rest added here); `NaturalSubject` describes the commodity or material undergoing the process (`IsLiving` distinguishes animate vs inert — cattle/timber vs wine/porcelain); `NaturalProcessIndustry` is a descriptive industry benchmark encoding `RatioBasisPoints = 10000 × TypicalLabourDaysCount / TypicalProductionDaysCount`. `market-service` gains five new `NaturalProcessKind` constants in `internal/circulation/natural_process.go`. Store: `ProductionTimeStore` interface (8 methods), `memory_production_time.go`, `mysql_production_time.go`. Migrations `00046_v2_ch13_production_time.sql` (DDL — 4 tables: `production_labour_gaps`, `natural_process_activations`, `natural_subjects`, `natural_process_industries`) and `00047_v2_ch13_seed.sql` (6 connected working periods + 6 gaps + 7 activations + 6 subjects + 5 industry benchmarks; seed IDs `5eed00000000000000130*`–`5eed00000000000000134*`). Endpoints: `POST/GET /v1/working-periods/{id}/production-labour-gap`, `POST/GET /v1/working-periods/{id}/natural-activations`, `POST/GET /v1/working-periods/{id}/natural-subject`, `POST/GET /v1/industry-benchmarks`. React panel `Ch13TimeOfProduction.tsx`: key-result insight box (production-time = labour-time + gap); stacked bar per natural-process working period (blue = labour, amber = gap) for six Marx fixtures (corn, wine, cattle, timber, porcelain, fallow); industry-benchmark table with inline ratio bars.

### Vol. II Ch. 15 — what was built

`simulation-engine` models the effects of commodity-price revolutions on the circuit of industrial capital from Capital Vol. II, Ch. 15:

- **Domain.** `circulation/price_revolution.go` — `ValueRevolutionEvent` (ID, industrial_capital_id, affecting enum `means_of_production | labour_power | output`, direction_pence, occurred_at, basis_points_change, revalued_capital_pence); `RevaluedCapital` (old/new advance pence, delta); `RealisedValueDelta` (expected vs actual realisation pence, delta); `InventoryRevaluation` (commodity_id, quantity_held, old/new unit pence, delta); `SpeculativeHold` (commodity_id, held_since, anticipated_direction int8); `CompoundCapitalAdjustment` (aggregate revaluation + realisation + inventory delta → net_effect_pence); `PriceFallCase` enum (`same_scale | expanded_scale | additional_stock`); `PriceRiseCase` enum (`partial_replacement | reduced_scale | credit_bridge`); `PriceCaseRecord`; `PriceRevolutionRecord` (composite of all sub-structs for a single event). `DeriveRevaluedCapital(event, oldAdvance)` applies the basis-points change to the old advance and returns a `RevaluedCapital`. `circulation/industrial_capital.go` extended with `BasisPointsChange int64` and `RevaluedCapitalPence Pence` on `ValueRevolutionEvent`.
- **Store.** `PriceRevolutionStore` interface with `CreatePriceRevolution`, `GetPriceRevolution`, `RecordPriceCase`, `RecordInventoryRevaluation`, `RecordSpeculativeHold`, `AggregateCompound`. Memory (`memory_price_revolution.go`) and MySQL (`mysql_price_revolution.go`) implementations. Migrations `00048_v2_ch15_price_revolution.sql` (DDL — `value_revolution_events`, `revalued_capitals`, `realised_value_deltas`, `inventory_revaluations`, `speculative_holds`, `price_case_records` tables) and `00049_v2_ch15_seed.sql` (Fixture A — Spinning Mill 1871 cotton −15% → £72 freed; Fixture B — Manchester Spinner 1857 output −10% → £60 loss; Fixture C — 5000-lb cotton stock revaluation −£100 paper loss; Fixture D — speculative hold on manchester-cotton-1857).
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/price-revolutions` (201 + Location, derives RevaluedCapital or RealisedDelta inline when advance/realisation fields provided), `GET /v1/price-revolutions/{id}` (200/404), `POST /v1/price-revolutions/{id}/price-case` (200), `POST /v1/inventory-revaluations` (201), `POST /v1/speculative-holds` (201), `GET /v1/price-revolutions/compound` (200, query: industrial_capital_id + period). api-gateway proxies `/v1/price-revolutions`, `/v1/price-revolutions/{rest...}`, `/v1/inventory-revaluations`, `/v1/speculative-holds`.
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 15 — The Effects of a Change of Prices" panel: Marx quote header; two-column layout with a create-form (fixture buttons for Spinning Mill 1871 and Manchester Spinner 1857, affecting selector, price-change pence, basis-points, conditional old-advance / expected-vs-actual fields) and a live event list (EventCard with colour-coded gain/loss border, capital-advance or realisation detail, price-case tag, inline inventory revaluation table); static case-reference section (fall cases vs rise cases grid).

### Vol. II Ch. 16 — what was built

`simulation-engine` models the turnover of variable capital and the resulting annual rate of surplus-value from Capital Vol. II, Ch. 16:

- **Domain.** `valorisation/variable_capital.go` — `VariableCapitalAdvance` (ID, industrial_capital_id, pence, turnover_period_minutes, turnovers_per_year_basis_points); `PerCircuitSurplusRate` (surplus_pence, variable_pence, basis_points = s/v × 10 000); `AnnualSurplusRate` (n_basis_points, per_circuit_basis_points, annual_basis_points = n × s/v encoded in bp); `AnnualSurplusRateContrast` (capital_a_id, capital_b_id, ratio_basis_points); `VariableCapitalReproduction` (total_variable_paid_annual_pence, total_surplus_annual_pence). Pure functions: `ComputePerCircuitRate`, `ComputeAnnualSurplusRate` (annual_bp = n_bp × pc_bp / 10 000), `ComputeAnnualSurplusRateContrast`, `ComputeVariableCapitalReproduction`.
- **Store.** `AnnualSurplusRateStore` interface (8 methods) embedded in the `Store` interface. Memory (`memory_valorisation.go`) and MySQL (`mysql_valorisation.go`) implementations. Migrations `00050_v2_ch16_annual_surplus_rate.sql` (DDL — `variable_capital_advances`, `per_circuit_surplus_rates`, `annual_surplus_rates`, `annual_surplus_rate_contrasts` tables; UNIQUE constraint on per_circuit_surplus_rates enforces one rate per advance) and `00051_v2_ch16_seed.sql` (Capitalist A: £500, n=10, annual=1000%; Capitalist B: £5000, n=1, annual=100%; Spinning Mill 1871: n=52, s/v=156%, annual=8112%; Locomotive Works: n≈4.3, s/v=80%, annual≈348%; seed IDs `5eed000000000000160[1-5]`).
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/variable-capital-advances` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/variable-capital-advances` (list), `GET /v1/variable-capital-advances/{id}`, `POST /v1/variable-capital-advances/{id}/per-circuit-rate` (201), `POST /v1/variable-capital-advances/{id}/compute-annual-rate?period=` (201; 409 if per-circuit rate not yet recorded), `GET /v1/annual-surplus-rates` (list, filterable by period), `POST /v1/annual-surplus-rate-contrasts` (201), `GET /v1/variable-capital-reproductions/{id}` (200; 409 if no per-circuit rate). api-gateway proxies all five path prefixes to simulation-engine.
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 16 — The Turnover of Variable Capital" panel: Marx quote header; §2 Capitalist A vs B side-by-side cards (same annual surplus £5 000, but 1 000% vs 100% annual rate); live formula slider (n × s/v with quadrupling demonstration); industry comparison cards — Locomotive Works (≈348%) vs Spinning Mill (8 112%) loaded from seed; interruption table showing how reducing n cuts the annual rate proportionally; SpinMill 1871 calculation card with live reproduction totals.

### Vol. II Ch. 17 — what was built

`simulation-engine` models the circulation of surplus-value from Capital Vol. II, Ch. 17:

- **Domain.** `reproduction/surplus_circulation.go` — `SurplusCirculation` (ID, period, TotalSurplusPence, RealisedSurplusPence, UnrealisedSurplusPence, RealisationSources); `RealisationSourceEntry` (SurplusCirculationID, Source, Pence, DeferredRealisationPence); `SocialCapitalAggregate` (Period, TotalAdvancedPence, TotalAnnualOutputPence, TotalAnnualSurplusPence, DepartmentIShareBP, DepartmentIIShareBP); `RealisationPuzzle` (SurplusCirculationID, PuzzleStatement, ResolvedInChapter). `RealisationSource` enum: `revenue_expenditure`, `accumulated_capital`, `gold_replacement`, `credit_deferred`, `foreign_trade`. Pure functions: `AddRealisationSource` (validates source, accumulates pence, sets deferred flag for credit/foreign); sentinel errors `ErrUnknownSource`, `ErrRealisationExceedsTotal`.
- **Store.** `SurplusCirculationStore` interface (4 methods: `CreateSurplusCirculation`, `GetSurplusCirculation`, `ListSurplusCirculations`, `RecordRealisationSource`, `SnapshotAggregate`, `UpsertAggregateShares`, `ListRealisationPuzzles`) embedded in the `Store` interface. `UpsertAggregateShares` writes the Department I/II share split onto the period's aggregate (creating the row if absent); it is invoked by the Ch. 20 (`AdvanceReproductionTick`) and Ch. 21 (`TickExtendedScheme`) tick endpoints, so the `DepartmentIShareBP`/`DepartmentIIShareBP` placeholders this chapter reserved are populated from the reproduction schemes rather than left at zero (#215). Memory (`memory_reproduction.go`) and MySQL (`mysql_reproduction.go`) implementations. Migrations `00052_v2_ch17_surplus_circulation.sql` (DDL — `surplus_circulations`, `realisation_source_entries` tables) and `00053_v2_ch17_seed.sql` (Marx 1871 illustration: £5 m total surplus, £4 m revenue expenditure 80%, £950 k accumulated capital 19%, £50 k gold reproduction 1%; seed IDs `5eed000000000000001701`–`5eed000000000000001703`).
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/reproduction/surplus-circulations` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/reproduction/surplus-circulations` (list), `GET /v1/reproduction/surplus-circulations/{id}`, `POST /v1/reproduction/surplus-circulations/{id}/realisation-source` (201; 422 if exceeds total; 400 if unknown source), `GET /v1/reproduction/social-aggregate?period=`, `GET /v1/reproduction/realisation-puzzles`. api-gateway proxies all four path prefixes to simulation-engine.
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 17 — The Circulation of Surplus-Value" panel: §1 Puzzle box with Marx quote and seeded puzzle statements; §2 Sankey-style bar chart — 80% revenue expenditure / 19% re-capitalised / 1% gold reproduction from the 1871 illustration; §3 Social capital aggregate widget (seed period 1871; the Dept I/II share split is unallocated until a Ch. 20/21 reproduction tick projects it onto the period's aggregate — #215); §4 live surplus-circulation cards loaded from API showing realisation progress bars and per-source breakdowns.

### Vol. II Ch. 18 — what was built

`simulation-engine` models the role of money-capital in social reproduction from Capital Vol. II, Ch. 18:

- **Domain.** `reproduction/money_supply.go` — enums: `CapitalDepartment` (`department_i`, `department_ii`), `ReservePurpose` (`wage_payment`, `means_of_production`, `capitalisation`, `inter_dept_settlement`), `InterDepartmentPurpose` (`means_of_production` for I→II and II→I flows). Structs: `MoneySupplyApportionment` (total social money split across Dept I reserve, Dept II reserve, wage-rotation fund, idle hoard); `DepartmentMoneyReserve` (per-department reserve with purpose); `CirculatingMoneyMass` (money stock, velocity in basis points, effective circulating value = stock × velocity / 10000); `WageRotationFund` (fund size, wage-cycle frequency, department); `InterDepartmentSettlement` (I→II and II→I flows). Pure functions: `ValidateApportionment` (checks Total == DeptI + DeptII + WageRotation + IdleHoard → `ErrApportionmentMismatch`), `ComputeEffectiveCirculatingValue` (stock × velocityBP / 10000). Sentinel errors: `ErrNegativeMoneyStock`, `ErrApportionmentMismatch`, `ErrZeroVelocity`.
- **Store.** `MoneyCapitalStore` interface (16 methods: Create/Get/List for all 5 types plus `CheckApportionmentBalance`) embedded in the `Store` interface. Memory (`memory_money_capital.go`) and MySQL (`mysql_money_capital.go`) implementations. Migrations `00054_v2_ch18_money_supply.sql` (DDL — 5 tables: `money_supply_apportionments`, `department_money_reserves`, `circulating_money_masses`, `wage_rotation_funds`, `inter_department_settlements`) and `00055_v2_ch18_seed.sql` (Marx 1865 two-department scheme: £1 000 total, 40/35/20/5 split, velocity 2× per year, weekly wage rotation; seed IDs `5eed000000000000001801`–`5eed000000000000001808`).
- **HTTP endpoints.** `POST /v1/reproduction/apportionments` (201), `POST /v1/reproduction/department-reserves` (201), `POST /v1/reproduction/circulating-money-mass` (201; effective value computed server-side), `POST /v1/reproduction/wage-rotation-funds` (201), `POST /v1/reproduction/inter-department-settlements` (201), `GET /v1/reproduction/apportionments/{id}/balance-check` (200 `{"balanced":bool,"apportionment_id":"..."}`).
- **React UI.** "Vol. II Ch. 18 — The Role of Money-Capital" panel: §1 apportionment pie (40/35/20/5 percentage bars, Dept I / Dept II / Wage Rotation / Idle Hoard); §2 M×V=P×Q calculator (money stock + velocity inputs, live effective-value output using integer basis-point maths); §3 wage-rotation toy (fund-size input, cycle-frequency selector Weekly/Monthly/Annual, displays turnovers/year); §4 two-department flow diagram (SVG arrows: I(v+s)→II and II(c)→I, prefiguring the Ch. 20 reproduction tables).

### Vol. II Ch. 14 — what was built

`market-service` deepens Ch. 5's two-span circulation model by decomposing each selling and buying phase into four sub-spans. New domain types in `internal/circulation/circulation_time_v2.go`: `SellingPhaseDetailed` (communication lag, buyer-search, bargaining, legal-settlement sub-spans with `ValidateSubSpans()` and `ValidateLagBound()`); `BuyingPhaseDetailed` (same four sub-spans); `DistanceLagRelation` (market-ID, distance in metres, lag in min/km, `MaxLagMinutes()`); `CirculationSpeedImprovement` (old/new lag, reason enum, effective date, `LagReduction()`); `AnnualSurplusPenalty` (`ComputePenalty() = IdealRate − ActualRate`); sentinel errors `ErrSubSpanMismatch` and `ErrLagExceedsMaximum`. Store: `CirculationTimeV2Store` interface (11 methods) embedded in `Store`, `Memory` and `MySQL` (`mysql_circulation_time_v2.go`) implementations. Migrations `00007_v2_ch14_circulation_time_v2.sql` (DDL — 5 tables: `selling_phase_details`, `buying_phase_details`, `distance_lag_relations`, `circulation_speed_improvements`, `annual_surplus_penalties`) and `00008_v2_ch14_seed.sql` (England–India 1850/1870 scenario: 2 selling phases, 2 detailed breakdowns, 3 distance-lag relations, 4 speed improvements — railway 1840, steamship 1845, telegraph 1870, telephone 1876 — and 1 annual surplus penalty; seed IDs `5eed000000000000001401`–`5eed000000000000001415`). Endpoints (7): `POST /v1/turnover-time/{id}/selling-phase/{phaseId}/detail`, `GET /v1/turnover-time/{id}/selling-phase/{phaseId}/detail`, `POST /v1/distance-lag`, `GET /v1/distance-lag`, `POST /v1/circulation-speed-improvements`, `GET /v1/circulation-speed-improvements`, `GET /v1/turnover-time/{id}/annual-surplus-penalty`. React panel `Ch14TimeOfCirculation.tsx`: §1 England–India telegraph slider lerping pre/post sub-span breakdowns (250-day → 1-day comm lag); §2 static 30-day selling-phase stacked breakdown bar; §3 chronological speed-improvement timeline (railway → steamship → telegraph → telephone); §4 penalty calculator (slider-driven basis-point penalty vs zero-CT ideal); §5 distance-lag relations table (fetched from API, falls back to seed constants).

### Vol. II Ch. 21 — what was built

`simulation-engine` adds the extended-reproduction domain from Capital Vol. II, Ch. 21.

- **Domain types.** `ExtendedReproductionScheme` (root, holds both departments + accumulation rates in bps + tick count); `Reinvestment` (per-cycle surplus split: Δc, Δv, consumed surplus, cycle number); `MultiPeriodScheme` (ordered list of scheme IDs for cross-period analysis); `CompositionShift` (c/v ratio in bps for both departments + social average per cycle); `ExtendedMoneyRequirement` (base + additional + total money to circulate growing commodity mass); `DepartmentIGrowthLead` (confirms Dept I grows faster than Dept II each cycle).
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeReinvestment(dept, rateBps)` — splits s into Δc/Δv/consumed using organic composition; integer arithmetic only. `ComputeNextPeriodCapital(dept, reinvest)` — advances c/v/s/total one cycle at same rate of exploitation. `ComputeExtendedBalance(deptI, deptII, rI, rII)` — checks keystone equation I(v + Δv + consumed_I) == II(c + Δc). `ComputeCompositionBps(dept)` — c/v in bps. `ComputeGrowthBps(prev, curr)` — total capital growth rate in bps. `ComputeExtendedMoneyRequirement(scheme, base)` — additional money proportional to accumulation rates.
- **Store.** `ExtendedReproductionStore` interface (11 methods). `TickExtendedScheme` uses `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on both department rows inside a transaction. `CreateMultiPeriodScheme` eagerly computes `CompositionShift` and `DepartmentIGrowthLead` rows for each consecutive scheme pair. `Memory` (`memory_extended_reproduction.go`) and `MySQL` (`mysql_extended_reproduction.go`) implementations. Migrations `00059_v2_ch21_extended_reproduction.sql` (DDL — 7 tables) and `00060_v2_ch21_seed.sql` (Marx's Year I fixture: Dept I 4000c+1000v+1000s at 50%, Dept II 1500c+750v+750s at 0%; seed IDs `5eed000000000000002101`–`5eed000000000000002107`).
- **HTTP endpoints (9).** `POST/GET /v1/reproduction/extended/schemes[/{id}]`; `POST /v1/reproduction/extended/schemes/{id}/departments`; `POST /v1/reproduction/extended/schemes/{id}/reinvestments`; `POST /v1/reproduction/extended/schemes/{id}/tick`; `GET /v1/reproduction/extended/schemes/{id}/money-requirement`; `POST /v1/reproduction/extended/multi-period`; `GET /v1/reproduction/extended/multi-period/{id}/composition-shift`; `GET /v1/reproduction/extended/multi-period/{id}/growth-lead`.
- **Ch. 17 integration (#215).** After each `TickExtendedScheme`, the handler projects the two departments' advanced capital (c+v) onto the period's Ch. 17 `SocialCapitalAggregate` via `UpsertAggregateShares`, so `DepartmentIShareBP`/`DepartmentIIShareBP` reflect the live scheme. The Ch. 20 simple-reproduction tick (`AdvanceReproductionTick`) does the same — populating the placeholders Ch. 17 reserved.
- **React panel.** `Ch21ExtendedReproduction.tsx` (6 widgets): ① canonical fixture table (4000c+1000v+1000s | 1500c+750v+750s with Δc/Δv/consumed columns and balance badge); ② live reinvestment-split widget with range sliders for accumulation rates and real-time balance check; ③ 5-year growth projection table showing Dept I consistently outpacing Dept II; ④ organic composition table (c/v in bps) rising each cycle — highlighted in amber when composition increases; ⑤ money-requirement breakdown (base + additional + total); ⑥ Vol. III bridge note linking rising organic composition to the tendential fall in the rate of profit (Ch. 13, Vol. III).

### Issue #214 — automatic tick scheduler

`simulation-engine` gains the automatic time-step orchestrator that five chapters' specs deferred to "when the tick loop is live" (V1 Ch.02/07/08/10, V2 Ch.21). This issue delivers the scheduler framework and wires the tickers whose advance operations already live in-process; it unblocks the per-domain behaviours (price oscillation, periodic depreciation, multi-period reproduction) tracked as follow-on issues.

- **Scheduler.** `engine/scheduler.go` — `Scheduler` runs a periodic loop (`time.Ticker`) at `SIM_TICK_INTERVAL` (a Go duration, default 5s), invoking each registered `Ticker` in deterministic registration order. `Start`/`Stop` are idempotent; `Stop` blocks until the in-flight pass finishes (graceful), and each pass runs on a context detached from the stop signal so a mid-pass `Stop` still completes its writes. `RunOnce` is the single shared code path for the loop and tests.
- **Tickers.** `engine/factory_ticker.go` advances every persisted `Factory` (Ch.15) one period per pass via `FactoryStore.AdvanceTick`; `engine/reproduction_ticker.go` advances every simple (Ch.20) and extended (Ch.21) reproduction scheme via `AdvanceReproductionTick`/`TickExtendedScheme`. Both continue past a single entity's failure and return a joined error, so one bad record never starves the pass. Each depends on a narrow local interface (`FactoryAdvancer` / `ReproductionAdvancer`) so the `engine` package stays free of the `store` import (which already imports `engine`).
- **Audit log.** `engine.ScheduledTick` (`id`, `sequence`, `occurred_at`, `duration_ms`, `tickers_run`, `entities_advanced`, `error_count`) is written once per pass. `EngineTickStore` with `Memory` (`memory_engine_tick.go`) and `MySQL` (`mysql_engine_tick.go`) implementations; migration `00061_engine_ticks.sql` creates the append-only `engine_ticks` table (not seeded — it is a runtime log).
- **HTTP endpoints (4).** `POST /v1/engine/start`, `POST /v1/engine/stop`, `GET /v1/engine/status` (running, tick number, last-tick-at, interval, registered tickers), `GET /v1/engine/ticks?limit=N` (recent passes, newest-first). All proxied through api-gateway. `SIM_TICK_AUTOSTART=true` starts the loop on boot; the default boots stopped (operator-controlled). The scheduler is stopped gracefully after the HTTP server drains on SIGTERM.
- **Out of scope (unblocked follow-ons).** Ch.08 periodic depreciation and Ch.10 working-day aggregation are not wired here — their one-period advance operations are not in-process in `simulation-engine` (working-day state lives in `agent-service`); the registry makes them drop-in once their own issues land. Redis-backed distributed scheduling (V1 Ch.02/07) is likewise deferred.

### Vol. III Ch. 1 — what was built

`finance-service` opens Capital Vol. III with the cost-price/profit domain from Ch. 1 — the first domain package in a service that was scaffolded empty in foundation Phase 3.

- **Domain types.** New package `services/finance-service/internal/profit/`. `LabourMinutes` (canonical value unit) plus the named magnitudes `ConstantCapital`, `VariableCapital`, `SurplusValue`. `CapitalOutlay` (consumed constant c with its fixed wear-and-tear / advanced-fixed breakdown, plus variable v) with `Validate()`. `CostPrice` (k = c + v, split into `FixedComponent` and `CirculatingComponent`) and `CostPriceID` / `NewCostPriceID()` (96-bit hex). `CommodityValueV3` (C = k + s). `ProfitForm` (C = k + s → C = k + p; p = s; `MystifiesOrigin`). `Profit` (selling-price − cost-price; `BelowValue` when sold under value but above cost-price).
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeCostPrice(outlay)` — k = c + v with the fixed/circulating split; `ComputeCommodityValue(k, s)` — C = k + s; `ComputeProfitForm(k, s)` — the C = k + p transformation; `ComputeProfit(sell, k, C)` — realised profit and the below-value flag.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `CreateCostPrice` / `GetCostPrice` / `ListCostPrices`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00001_v3_ch01_cost_price.sql` (the `cost_prices` table) and `00002_v3_ch01_seed.sql` (Marx's §1 fixtures: plain 400c+100v → k = 500, and the £1,200-fixed / £20-wear variant → fixed £20, circulating £480; seed IDs `5eed000000000000000101`–`...0102`). `mysql.go` adopts the `//go:embed migrations` + `pkgmysql.Migrate` pattern, replacing the empty Phase-3 scaffold.
- **HTTP endpoints (4).** `POST /v1/profit/cost-price` (compute k from an outlay and persist), `GET /v1/profit/cost-price`, `GET /v1/profit/cost-price/{id}`, `POST /v1/profit/profit-form` (the C = k + p transformation plus the ladder of profitable selling prices between k and C). All proxied through api-gateway — `/v1/profit/...` → finance-service:8085, the first live Vol. III route.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch01CostPriceAndProfit.tsx` (registered `v3-ch01`): a capital-decomposition form with Marx's two §1 presets; the cost-price k with a fixed/circulating split bar; the C = k + s → C = k + p formula with the "mystification" callout; and a selling-price table showing a profit is realised even below value. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `CostPriceResponse` / `ProfitFormResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `financeApi` (`computeCostPrice`, `getProfitForm`, `listCostPrices`, `getCostPrice`).
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** The rate of profit p′ = s/(c+v) is Ch. 2; the general (average) rate of profit and prices of production are Ch. 9.

### Vol. III Ch. 2 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the rate-of-profit domain from Capital Vol. III, Ch. 2 — the same surplus-value measured against two denominators, and the gap between the two rates.

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/profit/rate_of_profit.go`. `RateOfProfit` (struct carrying the surplus-value, the total capital C = c + v, and p′ = s/C in basis points); the named basis-point ratios `RateOfSurplusValue` (s′ = s/v) and `MystificationDegree` ((s′ − p′)/s′); `TotalCapital` (C = c + v); `ProfitRateID` / `NewProfitRateID()` (96-bit hex); `ProfitRateAnalysis` (a stored decomposition c + v + s with both rates and the mystification between them). Rates are integer basis points (10000 bp = 100%), keeping floating point out of the value layer.
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeRateOfProfit(s, c, v)` — p′ = s/C (zero total capital → zero rate); `ComputeRateOfSurplusValue(s, v)` — s′ = s/v (zero variable capital → zero rate); `ComputeProfitRateAnalysis(c, v, s)` — both rates plus the mystification, which is 0 when c = 0 (the rates coincide) and rises towards 10000 bp as constant capital dominates.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `CreateProfitRate` / `GetProfitRate` / `ListProfitRates`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00003_v3_ch02_rate_of_profit.sql` (the `profit_rates` table) and `00004_v3_ch02_seed.sql` (Marx's §2 fixtures: 400c+100v+100s → p′ 20% / s′ 100% / mystification 80%, and the limiting case v = C → p′ = s′ = 100% / mystification 0; seed IDs `5eed000000000000000201`–`...0202`).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/profit/rate` (compute both rates from a decomposition and persist), `GET /v1/profit/rate`, `GET /v1/profit/rate/{id}`. All proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/profit/rate` → finance-service:8085).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch02RateOfProfit.tsx` (registered `v3-ch02`): a c/v/s decomposition form with three presets (Marx's §2 example, the v = C equality case, and a high-composition contrast); side-by-side s′ and p′ rate cards; the C = c + v explanation; and a "mystification" bar showing how much of the rate of surplus-value the profit-form conceals. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `RateOfProfitV3` / `ProfitRateAnalysisResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `computeProfitRate` / `listProfitRates` / `getProfitRate` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** The relation of the rate of profit to the rate of surplus-value across the other variables (s′, turnover, wage of labour, etc.) is Ch. 3; the effect of turnover on the rate is Ch. 4; the general (average) rate of profit across branches is Ch. 8–9.

### Vol. III Ch. 3 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the variation domain from Capital Vol. III, Ch. 3 — Marx's master equation p′ = s′(v/C), which resolves the rate of profit into the rate of surplus-value and the value-composition of capital, and the systematic variation of those two factors.

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/profit/relation.go`. `ProfitRateFormula` (a decomposition expressed as the formula's variables: constant component `C`, variable component `V`, rate of surplus-value `SRate` in basis points) with methods `TotalCapital()` (= C + V), `ProfitRate()` (p′ = s′·v/C in bp) and `CompositionRatio()`. `CompositionRatio` (v/C in basis points — the value-composition read from the profit-rate side). `VariationCase` (named string + consts `CaseSConstantVCVariable`, `CaseSVariableVCConstant`, `CaseBothVariable`) with `DeriveVariationCase`. `ProfitRateComparison` (two capitals side by side; flags `EqualSurplusRate`, by which their rates of profit stand as their value-compositions). `VariationAnalysisID` / `NewVariationAnalysisID()` (96-bit hex). `VariationAnalysis` (the persisted entity: initial and changed decomposition, the case, old/new p′ and v/C in bp, and a `ProportionalChange` flag — true when p′ moved in exact proportion to s′, the §II case). Rates are integer basis points (10000 bp = 100%).
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeCompositionRatio(c, v)` — v/C in bp; `CompareProfitRates(first, second)` — both rates and value-compositions plus the equal-s′ flag; `ComputeVariationAnalysis(initial, changed)` — classifies the case, computes both rates and compositions, and detects a proportional change via the cross-product p′old·s′new == p′new·s′old.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `CreateVariation` / `GetVariation` / `ListVariations`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00005_v3_ch03_variation_analysis.sql` (the `variation_analyses` table) and `00006_v3_ch03_seed.sql` (Marx's §I.4 and §II.1 worked variations, all from the base 80c+20v at s′=100%: §I.4a c→100 p′ falls 20%→16⅔%; §I.4c c→60 p′ rises 20%→25%; §II.1 →160c+40v at s′=50% p′ falls 20%→10% in proportion; seed IDs `5eed000000000000000301`–`...0303`).
- **HTTP endpoints (4).** `POST /v1/profit/variation` (analyse and persist a variation), `GET /v1/profit/variation`, `GET /v1/profit/variation/{id}`, `POST /v1/profit/compare` (compute-only two-capital comparison). All proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/profit/variation`, `/v1/profit/compare` → finance-service:8085).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch03ProfitRateRelation.tsx` (registered `v3-ch03`): a live three-slider formula (c, v, s′) showing how p′ = s′(v/C) shifts as each factor moves; a variation analyser with Marx's three §I/§II presets that records the old→new p′ movement and a proportional-change badge; a two-capital comparison (equal vs unequal s′); and a recorded-variations table seeded from the store. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `ProfitRateFormulaV3` / `CompositionRatioV3` / `VariationAnalysisResponse` / `ProfitRateComparisonResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `analyseVariation` / `listVariations` / `getVariation` / `compareProfitRates` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** The annual rate of profit with the turnover factor n is Ch. 4; equalisation across branches into a general rate of profit is Ch. 8–9.

### Vol. III Ch. 4 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the turnover domain from Capital Vol. III, Ch. 4 — the correction the number of turnovers makes to the rate of profit. Across a year the variable capital yields its surplus once per turnover, so the annual rate of profit is p′ = s′·n·(v/C); two capitals of equal composition and equal s′ then have rates of profit "related inversely as their periods of turnover".

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/profit/turnover.go`. `TurnoverCount` (n, the whole number of turnovers per year), `AnnualSurplusValueRate` (S′ = s′·n in bp) and `VariableCapitalPerTurnover` (the variable capital advanced, the v in C — distinct from the year's wage bill vn the capitalist's books record) as named types. `AnnualProfitRate` (struct carrying the four factors — s′, n, v, C — and the rate p′ = s′·n·(v/C) in bp; mirrors Ch. 2's `RateOfProfit`). `TurnoverAnalysisID` / `NewTurnoverAnalysisID()` (96-bit hex). `TurnoverAnalysis` (the persisted entity: inputs C, V, SRate, N plus the annual rate of profit, the single-turnover rate s′·(v/C) it would be without turnover, the annual rate of surplus-value, and the annual wage bill vn) with a `Validate()` enforcing non-negative magnitudes and v ≤ C (new sentinel `ErrVariableExceedsTotal`). Rates are integer basis points (10000 bp = 100%).
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeAnnualProfitRate(s′, n, v, C)` — p′ = s′·n·(v/C) in bp; `ComputeTurnoverAnalysis(C, v, s′, n)` — assembles the annual rate, the single-turnover rate, S′ = s′·n and the wage bill vn. Multiplication precedes division so the integer result matches Marx's worked rates exactly (Capital A: 100%·2·20/100 = 40%).
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `CreateTurnoverAnalysis` / `GetTurnoverAnalysis` / `ListTurnoverAnalyses`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00007_v3_ch04_turnover_analysis.sql` (the `turnover_analyses` table) and `00008_v3_ch04_seed.sql` (Marx's §main worked capitals: A 100C/n=2 → 40%, B 200C/n=1 → 20% (equal composition, half the rate), I £11,000C/n=10 → 45 5/11%; seed IDs `5eed000000000000000401`–`...0403`).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/profit/turnover-analysis` (compute and persist), `GET /v1/profit/turnover-analysis`, `GET /v1/profit/turnover-analysis/{id}`. All proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/profit/turnover-analysis` → finance-service:8085).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch04TurnoverEffect.tsx` (registered `v3-ch04`): a live four-slider formula (C, v, s′, n) showing the single-turnover rate becoming the annual rate p′ = s′·n·(v/C), with S′ and the wage bill vn; Marx's three worked-capital presets (A/B/I) that persist and read back a single→annual card; a Manchester cotton-spinnery callout (Marx's real figures — C £12,500, v £318, s′ 153 11/13%, n ≈ 8½ → p′ ≈ 33.27%, S′ ≈ 1,307% — shown as prose since the model counts whole turnovers); and a recorded-analyses table seeded from the store. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `AnnualProfitRateV3` / `TurnoverAnalysisResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `createTurnoverAnalysis` / `listTurnoverAnalyses` / `getTurnoverAnalysis` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** The turnover of merchant capital and its distinct pricing effects is Ch. 18; credit shortening effective turnover time is Ch. 25–27.

### Vol. III Ch. 5 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the constant-capital-economy domain from Capital Vol. III, Ch. 5 — the rise in the rate of profit won by economising constant capital. Because p′ = s/(c+v) carries c in its denominator alone, any saving in c shrinks the denominator and lifts the rate while the surplus-value s and variable capital v stay put. Two enterprises with equal s = £1,000 on equal v = £1,000 make the point: c = £9,000 yields p′ = 10%, c = £11,000 only 8⅓%; the £2,000 economised is the whole difference.

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/profit/economy.go`. `EconomyKind` (named string + consts `KindSharedConditions`, `KindWasteReduction`, `KindWorkdayExtension`, `KindImprovedMachinery`, `KindRawMaterialSaving`, with `Valid()`). `ConstantCapitalEconomy` (the saving applied to a decomposition c/v/s, with `OldProfitRate()` = s/(c+v), `NewProfitRate()` = s/(c−saving+v), `ProfitRateGain()`, and a `Validate()` enforcing non-negative magnitudes, a known kind, and saving ≤ c — new sentinels `ErrSavingExceedsConstant`, `ErrUnknownEconomyKind`). Analytical helpers `SharedCondition` (§IV communal facility: `SavingPerParticipant()` = (SoloCost−SharedCost)/ParticipantCount), `WasteReduction` (§IV reclaimed waste: `ConstantCapitalSaving()` = RecoveredWaste×PricePerUnit), and `WorkdayExtensionEffect` (§I fixed capital spread over a longer day: `FixedChargePerHour()`, `ProfitRateGain()`). `EconomyAnalysisID` / `NewEconomyAnalysisID()` (96-bit hex). `EconomyAnalysis` (persisted entity: the economy plus the old/new rate of profit and the gain, in basis points). Rates are integer basis points (10000 bp = 100%).
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeEconomyAnalysis(ConstantCapitalEconomy)` assembles the old rate, new rate and gain; the unexported `profitRateBP(s, c, v)` forms s/(c+v) in bp with a zero-denominator guard. Multiplication precedes division so the integer result matches Marx's worked rates.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `CreateEconomyAnalysis` / `GetEconomyAnalysis` / `ListEconomyAnalyses`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00009_v3_ch05_economy.sql` (the `economy_analyses` table) and `00010_v3_ch05_seed.sql` (three worked economies: waste cotton 2,000c→1,800c (p′ 33.33%→35.71%), communal boiler 1,200c saving £100 (33.33%→35.29%), and the §I two-enterprise 11,000c→9,000c (8⅓%→10%); seed IDs `5eed000000000000000501`–`...0503`).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/profit/economy` (compute and persist), `GET /v1/profit/economy`, `GET /v1/profit/economy/{id}`. All proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/profit/economy` → finance-service:8085).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch05ConstantCapitalEconomy.tsx` (registered `v3-ch05`): a kind selector driving the form, c/v/s sliders, and §IV sub-calculators that derive the saving for the shared-conditions kind ((solo−shared)/participants) and the waste-reduction kind (recovered×price); an old→new p′ readout with the gain and the economy in c; a "Record this economy" action that persists; Marx's three worked-economy presets; and a recorded-economies table seeded from the store. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `EconomyKind` / `ConstantCapitalEconomyV3` / `EconomyAnalysisResponse` / `SharedConditionResponse` / `WasteReductionResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `createEconomyAnalysis` / `listEconomyAnalyses` / `getEconomyAnalysis` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** Economy in constant capital as a counteracting force to the falling rate of profit is Ch. 14; large-scale industry's imperatives for economy reach back to Vol. I, Ch. 15.

### Vol. III Ch. 6 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the price-fluctuation domain from Capital Vol. III, Ch. 6 — the movement in the rate of profit produced by a re-pricing of the raw materials that make up the circulating part of the constant capital. The surplus-value is indifferent to what the cotton cost, but p′ = s/(c+v) measures it against the whole advance: a rise in the raw-material price swells c and depresses p′, a fall shrinks c and lifts it. Only the circulating raw-material component moves; the fixed capital and v and s stay put. From the same £400 c + £100 v + £100 s (p′ = 20%), doubling a material-light capital's cotton drops p′ to 17.24%, while halving a material-heavy capital's cotton raises it to 32.26%.

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/profit/price_fluctuation.go`. `PriceFluctuationKind` (named string + consts `KindRise`, `KindFall`, with `Valid()`). `ConstantCapitalPriceEffect` (the capital split into a `FixedCapital` the price leaves untouched and an `OriginalMaterialCapital` that scales with `PriceFactor` — a percentage of the original price — plus v and s, with `OldProfitRate()`, `NewProfitRate()`, `NewConstantCapital()`, `Kind()`, and a `Validate()` enforcing non-negative magnitudes and a positive factor — new sentinel `ErrNonPositivePriceFactor`). `RawMaterialPriceFluctuation` (`Kind()`, `Factor()`). `CapitalRelease` / `CapitalTieUp` (§II: `Released()` / `TiedUp()` = StockMonths×StockUnits×price-delta — one magnitude with opposite sign). `SupplyRegulation` (§II: `Action()` returns expand on a fall, contract on a rise). `PriceFluctuationAnalysisID` / `NewPriceFluctuationAnalysisID()` (96-bit hex). `PriceFluctuationAnalysis` (persisted entity: the effect plus the direction and old/new rate of profit, in basis points).
- **Pure functions.** `ComputePriceFluctuationAnalysis(ConstantCapitalPriceEffect)` derives the direction and the old and new rates; reuses the package's `profitRateBP` (truncating, so the falling branch lands at 3225 bp, not the spec's rounded 3226).
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `CreatePriceFluctuationAnalysis` / `GetPriceFluctuationAnalysis` / `ListPriceFluctuationAnalyses`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00011_v3_ch06_price_fluctuation.sql` (the `price_fluctuation_analyses` table) and `00012_v3_ch06_seed.sql` (three worked fluctuations: cotton doubles 80→160 material (p′ 20%→17.24%), cotton halves 380→190 (20%→32.26%), cotton falls a quarter 360→270 (20%→24.39%); seed IDs `5eed000000000000000601`–`...0603`).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/profit/price-fluctuation` (compute and persist), `GET /v1/profit/price-fluctuation`, `GET /v1/profit/price-fluctuation/{id}`. All proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/profit/price-fluctuation` → finance-service:8085).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch06PriceFluctuation.tsx` (registered `v3-ch06`): fixed/material/price-factor/v/s sliders driving a live old→new p′ readout coloured by direction (a rise depresses, a fall lifts); a §II capital release/tie-up widget; Marx's three worked-fluctuation presets; a "Record this fluctuation" action; and a recorded-fluctuations table seeded from the store. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `PriceFluctuationKind` / `ConstantCapitalPriceEffectV3` / `PriceFluctuationAnalysisResponse` / `CapitalReleaseResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `createPriceFluctuationAnalysis` / `listPriceFluctuationAnalyses` / `getPriceFluctuationAnalysis` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** Price fluctuation as a counteracting influence on the falling rate of profit is Ch. 14; depreciation of existing capital stock during price falls is Ch. 15.

### Vol. III Ch. 7 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the supplementary-remarks domain from Capital Vol. III, Ch. 7, which closes Part I. Two threads: the **organic-composition channel** (with equal s and v, the capital carrying more constant capital shows the lower rate of profit — A at c = £9,000 shows 10%, B at c = £11,000 only 8⅓%, the £2,000 the whole 167 bp gap) and the **refutation of Rodbertus** (a nominal money-value change, or a proportional change at constant composition, leaves the rate of profit untouched; only a change in the organic composition moves it). A **Part I summary** gathers the rate-of-profit analyses recorded across Chs. 1–6 and names the determinants the part has established.

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/profit/supplementary.go`. `OrganicCompositionEffect` (two enterprises A/B, with `ProfitRateA()`, `ProfitRateB()`, `RateDifference()` = |A−B|). `CompositionEffectAnalysis` (persisted: the effect + the two rates + the gap). `MagnitudeChangeKind` (named string + consts `KindMoneyValueChange`, `KindProportionalChange`, `KindOrganicChange`, with `Valid()`). `CapitalMagnitudeChange` (`OldRate()`, `NewRate()` — equal to old for the two nominal kinds, moved for an organic change — and `RateUnchanged()`; `Validate()` adds the sentinel `ErrUnknownMagnitudeKind`). `MagnitudeChangeAnalysis` (persisted). `ProfitSurfaceAppearance` (records the inversion: profit appears to spring from the total capital and the capitalist's enterprise, its real source the unpaid labour in v alone). `PartISummary` + `PartIDeterminants()` + `PartISummaryID` / `NewPartISummaryID()`. `CompositionEffectID` / `MagnitudeChangeID` with their `New…ID()` (96-bit hex). Rates are integer basis points.
- **Pure functions.** `ComputeCompositionEffectAnalysis`, `ComputeMagnitudeChangeAnalysis`, `ComputePartISummary` (normalises a nil analyses slice to non-nil). Unexported `magnitudeRateBP(profit, capital)` and `absInt64`.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `Create/Get/List CompositionEffect` and `Create/Get/List MagnitudeChange`, with `Memory` and `MySQL` implementations. Migrations `00013_v3_ch07_supplementary.sql` (tables `composition_effects` + `magnitude_changes`) and `00014_v3_ch07_seed.sql` (two composition comparisons and three magnitude changes — money-value, proportional, organic; seed IDs `5eed…0701–0705`).
- **HTTP endpoints (7).** `POST`/`GET`/`GET {id}` for `/v1/profit/composition-effect` and `/v1/profit/magnitude-change`, plus `GET /v1/profit/summary` (a read-only Part I snapshot built from the rate-of-profit store). All proxied through api-gateway.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch07SupplementaryRemarks.tsx` (registered `v3-ch07`): a side-by-side organic-composition comparison (equal s/v, two c sliders, A vs B vs difference); a Rodbertus refutation panel with a kind selector and an old→new rate readout plus a green "rate unchanged" / red "rate moved" verdict; the Part I determinants list; and recorded-comparison and recorded-change tables seeded from the store. `web/src/types.ts` mirrors `OrganicCompositionEffectV3` / `CompositionEffectAnalysisResponse` / `MagnitudeChangeKind` / `CapitalMagnitudeChangeV3` / `MagnitudeChangeAnalysisResponse` / `PartISummaryResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds the composition, magnitude, and summary functions to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** The general (average) rate of profit and prices of production are Part II (Chs. 8–9); the counteracting causes of the falling rate are Ch. 14.

### Vol. III Ch. 8 — what was built

`finance-service` adds the new package `internal/avgprofit` for Capital Vol. III, Ch. 8, which opens Part II. The chapter's argument: with the same rate of surplus-value in every branch but different organic compositions (different v/C splits), the individual rate of profit p′ = s/C differs across branches. Living labour — and therefore surplus-value — is set in motion only by the variable capital. Sphere B (100c + 600v, s′ = 100%) sets six times as much living labour in motion per £700 as Sphere A (600c + 100v, s′ = 100%) and shows an individual p′ of 85.71% against A's 14.29%. The international comparison (European 84c/16v at s′ = 100%, p′ = 16%; Asian 16c/84v at s′ = 25%, p′ = 21%) shows that a lower rate of surplus-value is fully compatible with a higher individual rate of profit when the organic composition is lower enough. The equalisation of these divergent individual rates into a general rate is deferred to Ch. 9.

- **Domain types.** New package `services/finance-service/internal/avgprofit/`. `sphere.go`: named value types (`TotalCapital`, `ConstantCapital`, `VariableCapital`, `SurplusValue`, `RateOfSurplusValue`, `SphereProfitRate`, `LabourPowerIndex`) over `LabourMinutes int64`; `ProductionSphereID` (96-bit hex from crypto/rand); `ProductionSphere` struct with JSON snake_case tags; `Validate()` (sentinels `ErrNegativeMagnitude`, `ErrVariableExceedsTotal`). Package-private formula functions: `surplusValue` (s′·v/10000), `individualProfitRate` (round-half-up: `(s·10000 + C/2)/C` — so Sphere A yields 1429 bp not 1428), `variablePercent` (v·100/C), `labourPowerIndex` ((v·3600)·100/C). `ComputeProductionSphere` (pure constructor, assigns no ID/timestamp). `composition.go`: `TechnicalComposition` (Mp/Workers physical ratio), `OrganicComposition` (c/(c+v) value ratio with `Total()`, `ConstantPercent()`, `VariablePercent()`), `CompositionKind` consts (`KindHigher`/`KindAverage`/`KindLower`), `ClassifyComposition`.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `Create/Get/List ProductionSphere`, with `Memory` (map + sort desc) and `MySQL` (INSERT/SELECT + `scanProductionSphere` helper) implementations. Migrations `00015_v3_ch08_compositions.sql` (the `production_spheres` table) and `00016_v3_ch08_seed.sql` (four worked spheres: A, B, European, Asian; seed IDs `5eed…0801–0804`).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/avgprofit/spheres` (compute and persist), `GET /v1/avgprofit/spheres`, `GET /v1/avgprofit/spheres/{id}`. All proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/avgprofit/spheres` → finance-service:8085).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch08CompositionDifferences.tsx` (registered `v3-ch08`): preset buttons for Marx's four fixtures; sliders for C, v, s′ driving a live readout of constant capital, surplus-value, individual p′, v/C ratio, and labour-power index; an international-comparison panel (European vs Asian) making the point that lower s′ is compatible with higher p′; a "Record sphere" button; and a recorded-spheres table seeded from the store. Client p′ formula mirrors Go (Math.round for half-up). `web/src/types.ts` adds `ProductionSphereResponse` and `OrganicCompositionResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `listProductionSpheres`, `getProductionSphere`, `createProductionSphere` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** Equalisation of individual sphere rates into a general (average) rate of profit — Ch. 9. Surplus-profit of below-average composition — Ch. 10.

### Vol. III Ch. 9 — what was built

`finance-service` extends `internal/avgprofit` for Capital Vol. III, Ch. 9, which is the core chapter of Part II. The argument: competition of capitals equalises the divergent individual rates of profit (Ch. 8) into a single **general (average) rate** p̄′ = ΣS / ΣC — total social surplus-value divided by total social capital, capital-weighted. Each capital then draws **average profit** = its C × p̄′, regardless of how much surplus-value it itself produced. Commodities no longer sell at their values (c+v+s) but at **prices of production** = cost-price k + average profit. The transfer is zero-sum: higher-composition branches (price > value, deviation positive) are net receivers; lower-composition branches (price < value, deviation negative) are net donors. Marx's five-sphere fixture (all C=100, s′=100%): ΣS=110, ΣC=500, p̄′=2200 bp (22%), Σ prices = Σ values = 610, Σ deviations = 0.

- **Domain types.** New files in `services/finance-service/internal/avgprofit/`. `general_rate.go`: value types `CostPrice`, `ProductionPrice`, `CommodityValue`, `Profit`, `ValueDeviation` (signed), `GeneralProfitRateID`; package-private `generalProfitRate` (round-half-up, mirrors `individualProfitRate`); `GeneralProfitRate` struct + `Validate()` + `ComputeGeneralProfitRate`; `AverageProfit` + `ComputeAverageProfit` (Amount = C·rate/10000); `SocialCapitalAggregate` (compute-only) + `ComputeSocialCapitalAggregate` (derives per-sphere prices, checks conservation). `price_of_production.go`: `PriceOfProductionID`; `PriceOfProduction` + `Validate()` + `ComputePriceOfProduction` (avgProfit = k·rate/10000; price = k + avgProfit; deviation = price − value; composition from deviation sign — deviation>0→KindHigher, <0→KindLower, ==0→KindAverage); `ValuePriceDeviation` + `ComputeValuePriceDeviation`.
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `Create/Get GeneralProfitRate` and `Create/Get/List PriceOfProduction`. `memory.go` adds two maps (`generalRates`, `pricesOfProduction`). `mysql.go` adds INSERT/SELECT methods; `general_profit_rates` stores the Spheres slice as a JSON column (json.Marshal/Unmarshal); `scanGeneralProfitRate` and `scanPriceOfProduction` helpers. Migrations `00017_v3_ch09_general_rate.sql` (tables `general_profit_rates` + `prices_of_production`) and `00018_v3_ch09_seed.sql` (one general-rate row + five price-of-production rows for Marx's five spheres; seed IDs `5eed…0901–0906`).
- **HTTP endpoints (6).** `POST/GET /v1/avgprofit/general-rate[/{id}]` (compute and persist the general rate); `POST/GET/GET-list /v1/avgprofit/price-of-production[/{id}]` (compute and persist per-sphere prices); `POST /v1/avgprofit/social-aggregate` (pure compute, no store). All proxied through api-gateway.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch09GeneralRateOfProfit.tsx` (registered `v3-ch09`): editable five-sphere table (name, C, v, s′) with client-side preview of p̄′; "Compute general rate" button calls `computeSocialAggregate`; shows p̄′, per-sphere value vs price of production with signed deviation (green +, red −) and composition tag; conservation banner (Σ prices = Σ values = X; Σ deviations = 0); seeded prices-of-production table loaded from store. `web/src/types.ts` adds `GeneralProfitRateResponse`, `PriceOfProductionResponse`, `ValuePriceDeviationResponse`, `SocialCapitalAggregateResponse`; `web/src/api.ts` adds `computeGeneralProfitRate`, `createPriceOfProduction`, `listPricesOfProduction`, `getPriceOfProduction`, `computeSocialAggregate` to `financeApi`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** Wage-fluctuation effect on prices of production — Ch. 11. Tendency of the rate to fall — Ch. 13.

### Vol. III Ch. 10 — what was built

`finance-service` extends `internal/avgprofit` for Capital Vol. III, Ch. 10. The argument: competition is the *mechanism* that equalises divergent sphere rates toward the general rate. Capital flows into above-average branches (inflow) and out of below-average branches (outflow); the resulting supply shifts move market prices until rates converge. Within a branch the **market value** is set by the production conditions of the *bulk* of output. A firm producing below the market value (higher individual productivity) sells at the market value and pockets the difference as **surplus-profit** — the signed extra: Amount = (MarketValue − IndividualValue) × OutputQty.

- **Domain types.** Two new files in `services/finance-service/internal/avgprofit/`. `market_value.go`: value types `MarketPriceAmount`, `IndividualValue`; `MarketValueID` + `NewMarketValueID()`; `MarketValue` struct + `Validate()` + `ComputeMarketValue` (Value = bulk); `MarketPrice` value object + `ComputeMarketPrice` (Deviation = amount − marketValue); `SurplusProfitID` + `NewSurplusProfitID()`; `SurplusProfit` struct (Amount signed `Profit`) + `Validate()` + `ComputeSurplusProfit` (Amount = (market−individual)×qty). `equalisation.go`: `CapitalFlowDirection` string type + constants `KindInflow/KindOutflow/KindStable`; package-private `capitalFlowDirection`; `CapitalFlow` value object + `ComputeCapitalFlow` (rate-based when generalRate≠0, else price-based); `EqualisationID` + `NewEqualisationID()`; `Equalisation` struct + `Validate()` + `ComputeEqualisation` (IsConverging = initialRate≠targetRate).
- **Store.** `Store` interface gains `Create/Get MarketValue`, `Create/Get SurplusProfit`, `Create/Get Equalisation`. `memory.go` adds three maps. `mysql.go` adds INSERT/SELECT methods for the three tables; `equalisations` stores flat columns and rebuilds `Flow` via `ComputeCapitalFlow` on scan. Migrations `00019_v3_ch10_market_equalisation.sql` (tables `market_values`, `surplus_profits`, `equalisations`) and `00020_v3_ch10_seed.sql` (cotton market-value, high-productivity firm surplus-profit, cotton equalisation; seed IDs `5eed…1001–1003`).
- **HTTP endpoints (6).** `POST/GET /v1/avgprofit/market-value[/{id}]`; `POST/GET /v1/avgprofit/surplus-profit[/{id}]`; `POST /v1/avgprofit/capital-flow` (builds Equalisation, returns 201 + Location → `/v1/avgprofit/equalisation/{id}`); `GET /v1/avgprofit/equalisation/{id}`. All proxied through api-gateway.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch10Equalisation.tsx` (registered `v3-ch10`): capital-flow visualiser (sphere rate vs general rate → inflow/outflow/stable arrow + convergence note); surplus-profit calculator (individual value, market value, qty → signed amount in green/red); market-value panel (bulk/best/worst → value = bulk). On mount POSTs the cotton/high-productivity-firm pre-seed fixtures so the panel is populated. `web/src/types.ts` adds `MarketValueResponse`, `SurplusProfitResponse`, `CapitalFlowResponse`, `EqualisationResponse`; `api.ts` adds `createMarketValue`, `getMarketValue`, `computeSurplusProfit`, `getSurplusProfit`, `createCapitalFlow`, `getEqualisation`.
- **Deferred (later Vol. III chapters).** Wage-fluctuation effect on prices of production — Ch. 11. Tendency of the rate to fall — Ch. 13.

### Vol. III Ch. 11 — what was built

`finance-service` extends `internal/avgprofit` for Capital Vol. III, Ch. 11. The argument: a general wage rise lowers the general rate of profit but does not uniformly raise prices of production. Prices shift in opposite directions by organic composition — higher-composition (capital-intensive) spheres see prices fall; lower-composition (labour-intensive) spheres see prices rise; the average-composition sphere is unchanged. Living labour (v+s) per sphere is conserved across the wage change, so total price of production is conserved (Σ price changes = 0).

All price/cost magnitudes in this chapter are integer **centi-units** (×100). A 25% wage change on v=50 yields fractional intermediate values (62.5); centi-units keep all arithmetic exact in int64.

- **Domain types.** New file `services/finance-service/internal/avgprofit/wage_effect.go`.
