### Ch. 47 — Genesis of Capitalist Ground-Rent

`finance-service` extends `internal/rent/` (Vol. III Ch. 47, Part VI). Chapter 47 traces ground-rent back through its pre-capitalist history, identifying three successive forms before capitalist ground-rent proper emerges, and examines the small peasant proprietor as the transitional figure in whom all three class revenues are conflated. **COMPLETES Part VI** (Ch. 37–47).

- **Domain types.** `RentFormStage int` enum 1–4 (1=LabourRent, 2=ProductRent, 3=MoneyRent, 4=CapitalistGroundRent). `RentHistoricalFormID` + `NewRentHistoricalFormID()`, `HistoricalRentTransitionID` + `NewHistoricalRentTransitionID()`, `SmallPeasantProductionID` + `NewSmallPeasantProductionID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `RentHistoricalForm` (persisted; `Stage RentFormStage`, `Name string`, `CoercionKind string`, `SurplusForm string`, `ExampleRegion string`, `ExamplePeriod string`). `HistoricalRentTransition` (persisted; `FromStage`, `ToStage RentFormStage`, `Preconditions string`, `DrivingForce string`; invariant: FromStage < ToStage). `SmallPeasantProduction` (persisted; `ParcelID`, `TotalIncomeBP`, `RentComponentBP`, `ProfitComponentBP`, `WagesComponentBP int64`, `IsConflated bool`). Pure function: `ComputeTotalIncome(rent, profit, wages int64) int64` = rent+profit+wages.
- **Store.** Interface gains six methods: `CreateRentHistoricalForm`/`ListRentHistoricalForms`, `CreateHistoricalRentTransition`/`ListHistoricalRentTransitions`, `CreateSmallPeasantProduction`/`ListSmallPeasantProductions`. Migrations `00093_v3_ch47_genesis_schema.sql` (tables `rent_historical_forms`, `historical_rent_transitions`, `small_peasant_productions`) and `00094_v3_ch47_genesis_seed.sql` (seed IDs: `5eed000000000000004700` labour-rent/corvée/Danubian principalities/9th–14th c; `5eed000000000000004701` product-rent/harvest-share/medieval England/12th–15th c; `5eed000000000000004702` money-rent/money-sum/France/15th–18th c; `5eed000000000000004703` capitalist-ground-rent/market/England/19th c; `5eed000000000000004704` transition labour→product; `5eed000000000000004705` transition money→capitalist; `5eed000000000000004706` French peasant rent=2/profit=1/wages=3/income=6/conflated=true; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).

### Ch. 52 — Classes

`finance-service` extends `internal/revenue/` (Vol. III Ch. 52, Part VII) — the final, unfinished chapter of *Capital*. Marx names the three great classes (wage-labourers→wages, capitalists→profit, landowners→rent), then poses but never answers "what constitutes a class?" — a shared relation to the means of production, not the income form alone. **COMPLETES Part VII and Vol. III.**

- **Domain types.** `SocialClassID`, `ClassIncomeSourceID`, `ClassTendencyID`, `ClassAmbiguityID` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `SocialClass` (`Name`, `RelationToMeansOfProduction`, `PrimaryRevenueForm`, `HistoricalPrecondition`). `ClassIncomeSource` (`ClassID`, `RevenueForm`, `SurplusValueComponentBP` — 0 for wages, >0 for profit/rent). `ClassTendency` (`ClassID`, `Description`, `Direction`). `ClassAmbiguity` (`IntermediateGroup`, `IncomeSource`, `WhyNotAClass`). Pure function: `ValidClassTendencyDirection` (expansion/concentration/dissolution/subordination).
- **Store.** Interface gains nine methods (`CreateSocialClass`/`GetSocialClass`/`ListSocialClasses`, Create/List for income sources, tendencies, ambiguities; impls in `revenue_classes_memory.go` + `revenue_classes_mysql.go`). Migrations `00103_v3_ch52_classes_schema.sql` (tables `social_classes`, `class_income_sources`, `class_tendencies`, `class_ambiguities`) and `00104_v3_ch52_classes_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000005200`–`5209`: three classes, three income sources wages/profit/rent, three tendencies, the officials ambiguity with `why_not_a_class = "manuscript_breaks_off"`; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `GET /v1/revenue/classes` (`{items:[...]}` never null), `GET /v1/revenue/classes/{id}` (404 via `errors.Is(ErrNotFound)`; returns `{class, income_sources, tendencies}`), `GET /v1/revenue/class-ambiguities` (`{items:[...]}` never null). New api-gateway proxy rules for the two path prefixes.

### Ch. 51 — Distribution Relations and Production Relations

`finance-service` extends `internal/revenue/` (Vol. III Ch. 51, Part VII). Chapter 51 establishes that distribution relations (wages, profit, rent) are the "other side" of production relations and share their historically transitory character; each distribution form rests on a production relation and corresponds to a component of new value (wages→v, profit/rent→s). A crisis arises when the productive forces contradict the existing social form.

- **Domain types.** `ProductionRelationBasisID`, `DistributionRelationID`, `HistoricalTransienceID`, `ProductiveForceContradictionID` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `ProductionRelationBasis` (`Name`, `CharacteristicFeature`, `IsHistoricallySpecific bool`). `DistributionRelation` (`Form`, `ProductionBasisID`, `ApparentCharacter`, `RealCharacter`, `CorrespondsToSurplusForm`). `HistoricalTransience` (`DistributionFormID`, `ModeOfProduction`, `Precondition`, `SucceedingForm`). `ProductiveForceContradiction` (`ProductiveForce`, `SocialForm`, `ContradictionNature`, `CrisisSignal`) — qualitative, no value calculation.
- **Store.** Interface gains eight methods (Create/List for each of the four types; impls in `revenue_distribution_memory.go` + `revenue_distribution_mysql.go`). Migrations `00101_v3_ch51_distribution_relations_schema.sql` (tables `production_relation_bases`, `distribution_relations`, `historical_transiences`, `productive_force_contradictions`) and `00102_v3_ch51_distribution_relations_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000005100`–`5107`: three bases wage-labour/capital/landed-property, three relations wages/profit/rent, one transience, one contradiction; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (5).** `GET /v1/revenue/production-bases`, `GET /v1/revenue/distribution-relations`, `GET /v1/revenue/historical-transiences`, `POST /v1/revenue/productive-force-contradictions` (422 when productive_force/social_form empty), `GET /v1/revenue/productive-force-contradictions` — all lists `{items:[...]}` never null. New api-gateway proxy rules for the four path prefixes.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch51Distribution.tsx` (registered `v3-ch51`, status pending→done): intro on distribution-as-production relations; tables for production bases, distribution relations, historical transience; a productive-force contradiction builder + list. CSS prefix `v3-ch51`.

### Ch. 50 — Illusions Created by Competition

`finance-service` extends `internal/revenue/` (Vol. III Ch. 50, Part VII). Chapter 50 shows how competition (market prices, buying and selling) hides the origin of value: cost-price (c+v) appears as the commodity's "real value" and profit appears to arise in the sale, while at the social level new value (v+s) is the annual revenue and constant capital c appears to reproduce itself automatically through exchange between departments.

- **Domain types.** `CompetitionIllusionID`, `CostPriceFetishID`, `AnnualRevenueAccountID`, `ValueAppearanceContrastID` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `CompetitionIllusion` (`Name`, `SurfaceAppearance`, `RealRelation`, `ProducedBy`). `CostPriceFetish` (`ConstantCapitalBP`, `VariableCapitalBP`, `CostPriceBP`, `ProfitBP`, `ActualValueBP int64`). `AnnualRevenueAccount` (`NewValueCreatedLM`, `WagesShareLM`, `ProfitAndRentShareLM`, `ConstantCapitalLM`, `TotalSocialProductLM int64`). `ValueAppearanceContrast` (`IllusionID`, `Surface`, `Reality`). Pure functions: `ComputeCostPrice`=c+v, `ComputeActualValue`=cost+profit, `ComputeNewValueCreated`=wages+profit&rent, `ComputeTotalSocialProduct`=c+new.
- **Store.** Interface gains eight methods (Create/List for each of the four types; impls in `revenue_illusions_memory.go` + `revenue_illusions_mysql.go`). Migrations `00099_v3_ch50_competition_illusions_schema.sql` (tables `competition_illusions`, `cost_price_fetishes`, `annual_revenue_accounts`, `value_appearance_contrasts`) and `00100_v3_ch50_competition_illusions_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000005000`–`5006`: three illusions, one cost-price fetish 70/30→100/120, one annual account 6000/1500/1500→3000/9000, two contrasts; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (4).** `GET /v1/revenue/competition-illusions` (`{items:[...]}` never null), `POST /v1/revenue/cost-price-fetishes` (computes cost-price + actual value), `POST /v1/revenue/annual-accounts` (computes new value + total social product), `GET /v1/revenue/value-contrasts` (`{items:[...]}` never null). New api-gateway proxy rules for the four path prefixes.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch50Illusions.tsx` (registered `v3-ch50`, status pending→done): intro on competition illusions; (1) illusions table; (2) cost-price fetish builder (c/v/profit, live cost/actual preview); (3) annual revenue account builder (c/wages/profit&rent, live new/total); (4) surface-vs-reality contrasts table. CSS prefix `v3-ch50`.

### Ch. 49 — Concerning the Analysis of the Process of Production

`finance-service` extends `internal/revenue/` (Vol. III Ch. 49, Part VII). Chapter 49 analyses the value of the annual product as c + v + s and demolishes Adam Smith's "adding-up" dogma: only v + s is newly created value, while constant capital c is transferred and must be reproduced, not resolved into revenue. Surplus-value s is the ceiling from which profit, interest, and rent are drawn.

- **Domain types.** `AnnualValueCompositionID`/`NewAnnualValueCompositionID()`, `RevenueLimitID`/`NewRevenueLimitID()`, `SurplusValuePartitionID`/`NewSurplusValuePartitionID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `AnnualValueComposition` (persisted; `ConstantCapitalLabourMinutes`, `VariableCapitalLabourMinutes`, `SurplusValueLabourMinutes`, `TotalValueLabourMinutes`, `NewValueLabourMinutes int64`). `RevenueLimit` (persisted; `CompositionID`, `MaxDistributableRevenueLM`, `ConstantCapitalReplacement int64`). `SurplusValuePartition` (persisted; `CompositionID`, `TotalSurplusValueLM`, `ProfitEnterpriseLM`, `InterestLM`, `GroundRentLM`, `ResidualSurplusLM int64`). Pure functions: `ComputeTotalValue(c,v,s)`=c+v+s, `ComputeNewValue(v,s)`=v+s, `DeriveRevenueLimit(comp)` (max = new value, replacement = c), `SumSurplusPartition`, `ValidateRevenueLimit(comp, partition)` (parts sum to total; total ≤ produced s).
- **Store.** Interface gains six methods: `CreateAnnualValueComposition`/`GetAnnualValueComposition`, `CreateRevenueLimit`/`GetRevenueLimitByComposition`, `CreateSurplusValuePartition`/`ListSurplusValuePartitions` (impls in `revenue_analysis_memory.go` + `revenue_analysis_mysql.go`). Migrations `00097_v3_ch49_revenue_composition_schema.sql` (tables `annual_value_compositions`, `revenue_limits`, `surplus_value_partitions`) and `00098_v3_ch49_revenue_composition_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000004900` composition 6000/1500/1500, `…4901` limit max 3000/replacement 6000, `…4902` partition 900/300/300; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/revenue/annual-compositions` (201 + Location; computes total/new, derives + stores the revenue limit; returns `{composition, revenue_limit}`), `GET /v1/revenue/annual-compositions/{id}` (404 via `errors.Is(ErrNotFound)`), `POST /v1/revenue/surplus-partitions` (404 unknown composition, 422 if the partition exceeds produced surplus-value — Smith's error). New api-gateway proxy rules for the two path prefixes.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch49Analysis.tsx` (registered `v3-ch49`, status pending→done): intro on c+v+s and Smith's error; (1) annual-composition builder — c/v/s inputs (defaults 6000/1500/1500), live total/new preview, result shows the derived revenue limit; (2) surplus-value partition builder — composition_id (auto-filled from the created composition), profit-enterprise/interest/ground-rent/residual, live sum, 422 when exceeding s. CSS prefix `v3-ch49`.

### Ch. 48 — The Trinity Formula

`finance-service` adds a new package `internal/revenue/` (Vol. III Ch. 48, Part VII). Chapter 48 opens Part VII with the Trinity Formula — Capital→interest, Land→ground-rent, Labour→wages — the ideological surface in which all surplus-value appears to spring from three independent sources. The model records each branch's fetishised apparent yield against the portion of surplus-value it really represents, showing that profit, interest, and rent are a partition of the surplus-labour pumped from wage-workers, while wages merely recover variable capital. **OPENS Part VII.**

- **Domain types.** `RevenueSourceKind int` enum 1–3 (1=Capital, 2=Land, 3=Labour) with `IsValid()`. `RevenueStreamID`/`NewRevenueStreamID()`, `TrinityFormulaID`/`NewTrinityFormulaID()`, `RevenueFetishFormID`/`NewRevenueFetishFormID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `RevenueStream` (persisted; `Source RevenueSourceKind`, `ApparentRevenueBP`, `ActualSourceBP int64`, `IsFetishised bool`). `TrinityFormula` (persisted; `CapitalStreamID`, `LandStreamID`, `LabourStreamID string`, `TotalSurplusValueBP`, `TotalApparentRevenueBP int64`). `RevenueFetishForm` (persisted; `Source`, `SurfaceFormula`, `RealRelation`, `MystificationKind`). Pure functions: `SumApparentRevenueBP`, `SumActualSourceBP`, `VariableCapitalBP(streams)` (= wages of the labour stream).
- **Store.** Interface gains six methods: `CreateRevenueStream`/`ListRevenueStreams`, `CreateTrinityFormula`/`GetTrinityFormula`, `CreateRevenueFetishForm`/`ListRevenueFetishForms` (impls in new `revenue_memory.go` + `revenue_mysql.go`). Migrations `00095_v3_ch48_trinity_schema.sql` (tables `revenue_streams`, `trinity_formulas`, `revenue_fetish_forms`) and `00096_v3_ch48_trinity_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000004800` formula s=23/apparent=103; `…4801` capital stream apparent=20/actual=20; `…4802` land apparent=3/actual=3; `…4803` labour apparent=80/actual=0; `…4804`–`…4806` the three fetish forms; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/revenue/trinity-formulas` (201 + Location; creates the three streams, computes totals, binds the formula; 422 if the labour stream claims surplus-value), `GET /v1/revenue/trinity-formulas/{id}` (404 via `errors.Is(ErrNotFound)`), `GET /v1/revenue/fetish-forms` (`{items:[...]}` never null). New api-gateway proxy rules for the `/v1/revenue/` prefix (first Part VII routes).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch48Trinity.tsx` (registered `v3-ch48`, status pending→done): intro on the trinity fetish and the partition of surplus-value; (1) trinity-formula builder — capital/land apparent+actual, labour wages (actual locked to 0), live totals (apparent / surplus-value / new value s+v), submit shows the bound formula totals + a streams table; (2) revenue fetish forms list table (surface formula vs real relation vs mystification). Defaults = seed fixture (20/20, 3/3, 80/0 → apparent 103, s 23). CSS prefix `v3-ch48`. **OPENS Part VII.**

### Ch. 44 — Differential Rent Also on the Worst Cultivated Soil

`finance-service` extends `internal/rent/` (Vol. III Ch. 44, Part VI). Chapter 44 resolves the apparent paradox that soil&nbsp;A — the worst, rent-free, regulating soil — can nevertheless yield differential rent. Marx identifies three mechanisms.

- **Domain types.** `RentEmergenceMechanism int` enum: 1 = better soil's sub-average additional investment becomes the new price-regulator, opening a gap above A; 2 = increasing productivity of additional capital on A lowers the regulating price so A earns a surplus-profit; 3 = decreasing productivity raises A's average price of production above the old regulating price. `RentOnWorstSoilID` + `NewRentOnWorstSoilID()`, `SoilImprovementCapitalID` + `NewSoilImprovementCapitalID()`, `RegulatingPriceShiftID` + `NewRegulatingPriceShiftID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `RentOnWorstSoil` (persisted; `Mechanism RentEmergenceMechanism`, `OldPriceOfProductionBP int64`, `NewPriceOfProductionBP int64`, `RentPerAcreBP int64`). `SoilImprovementCapital` (persisted; `CapitalInvestedBP int64`, `ProductivityGainBP int64`, `AmortisationYears int`, `AmortisedYears int`; `IsAmortised() bool` returns `AmortisedYears >= AmortisationYears`). `RegulatingPriceShift` (persisted; `FromGrade int`, `ToGrade int`, `OldPriceBP int64`, `NewPriceBP int64`). `ComputeWorstSoilRentPerAcre(oldBP, newBP int64) int64` pure fn: `max(0, newBP−oldBP)`. Price scale: £1 = 10,000 bp throughout.
- **Store.** Interface gains six methods: `CreateRentOnWorstSoil`/`ListRentOnWorstSoils`, `CreateSoilImprovementCapital`/`ListSoilImprovementCapitals`, `CreateRegulatingPriceShift`/`ListRegulatingPriceShifts`. Migrations `00087_v3_ch44_worst_soil_schema.sql` (tables `rent_on_worst_soils`, `soil_improvement_capitals`, `regulating_price_shifts`) and `00088_v3_ch44_worst_soil_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000004400` mechanism-1 rent old=30000 new=35000 rent=5000, `5eed000000000000004401` mechanism-3 rent old=30000 new=35000 rent=5000, `5eed000000000000004402` drainage improvement capital=3000 gain=500 amortisation=15 amortised=0, `5eed000000000000004403` A→B price shift old=30000 new=35000; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (6).** `POST /v1/rent/worst-soil-rents` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/rent/worst-soil-rents` (`{items:[...]}` never null), `POST /v1/rent/soil-improvements` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/rent/soil-improvements` (`{items:[...]}` never null), `POST /v1/rent/regulating-price-shifts` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/rent/regulating-price-shifts` (`{items:[...]}` never null). New api-gateway proxy rules for all three path prefixes.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch44WorstSoilRent.tsx` (registered `v3-ch44`, status pending→done): intro on three mechanisms and amortisation of permanent improvements; (1) Rent on worst soil form — parcel_id default `5eed000000000000003700`, mechanism select (1/2/3 with labels), old_price_of_production_bp default 30000, new_price_of_production_bp default 35000 → `createRentOnWorstSoil`; list table (mechanism label, old/new price as £, rent/acre as £); note "rent = max(0, new − old)"; (2) Soil improvement capital form — parcel_id, capital_invested_bp default 3000, productivity_gain_bp default 500, amortisation_years default 15, amortised_years default 0 → `createSoilImprovementCapital`; list table with amortised status (`amortised_years >= amortisation_years` → "Dissolved into DR"); (3) Regulating price shifts form — from_grade select A–D (1–4), to_grade select A–D, old_price_bp default 30000, new_price_bp default 35000 → `createRegulatingPriceShift`; list table. CSS prefix `v3-ch44`. All prices displayed as £ = bp/10000.

### Ch. 43 — Differential Rent II, Third Case (Rising Price of Production)

`finance-service` extends `internal/rent/` (Vol. III Ch. 43, Part VI) — Engels completed this chapter from Marx's notes (Marx left only the title). DR&nbsp;II under a **rising price of production** occurs when additional capital invested on the same soil yields below the average return, or when new inferior soil must be cultivated. In most cases total rent doubles or more when capital doubles. Differential rent is an arithmetic progression of rent differences measured from the rent-free regulating soil. Units throughout are **shillings&nbsp;(s)** and **bushels&nbsp;(bu)**, following Engels's own tables.

- **Domain types.** `RisingPriceVariant int` enum (1–5, covering Engels's five eventualities: A constant, B decreasing, B increasing, A doubling, B mixed). `RisingPriceDR2OutcomeID` + `NewRisingPriceDR2OutcomeID()`, `RentSequenceID` + `NewRentSequenceID()`, `EngelsTableEntryID` + `NewEngelsTableEntryID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `RisingPriceDR2Outcome` (persisted; `Variant RisingPriceVariant` stored as `rising_variant`; `TotalCapitalShillings int64`, `TotalRentShillings int64`, `TotalOutputBushels int64`, `PricePerBushelShillings int64`). `RentSequence` (persisted; `BaseRent int64`, `Increment int64` stored as `rent_increment`, `NumGrades int`). `EngelsTableEntry` (persisted; `TableNumber int`, `SoilGradeLabel string`, `OutputBushels int64`, `RentShillings int64`, `CapitalShillings int64`). `ComputeRentSequence(base, increment int64, numGrades int) []int64` pure fn: `rent[g] = base + (g-1)*increment`. Table XI canonical sequence: [0, 12, 24, 36, 48] = 120s total.
- **Store.** Interface gains six methods: `CreateRisingPriceDR2Outcome`/`ListRisingPriceDR2Outcomes`, `CreateRentSequence`/`ListRentSequences`, `CreateEngelsTableEntry`/`ListEngelsTableEntries`. MySQL column names `rising_variant` (avoids collision with `variant`) and `rent_increment` (avoids reserved-word risk). ### Ch. 41 — Differential Rent II, First Case (Constant Price of Production)

`finance-service` extends `internal/rent/` (Vol. III Ch. 41, Part VI) — DR&nbsp;II under a **constant regulating price of production**, meaning soil&nbsp;A remains the worst cultivated soil and continues to set the market price throughout. Under this constant-price condition, every additional investment of capital on the better soils raises absolute rent per acre — whether the productivity of the added capital relative to the first tranche is proportional (Case&nbsp;II), falls (Case&nbsp;III), or rises (Case&nbsp;IV). The chapter also demonstrates that **intensive cultivation** (concentrating capital on the same better parcel) raises rent per acre more than **extensive cultivation** (dispersing the same capital across a wider area) when total capital and total rental are held equal.

- **Domain types.** `CapitalProductivityCase int` enum (1=`CaseAverageOnly`, 2=`CaseProportional`, 3=`CaseDecreasingRate`, 4=`CaseIncreasingReturn`). `DifferentialRentIIOutcomeID` + `NewDifferentialRentIIOutcomeID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `DifferentialRentIIOutcome` (persisted; `ParcelID`, `ProductivityCase` stored as `productivity_case` column to avoid MySQL reserved word `case`, `AdditionalCapitalBP int64`, `NewRentPerAcreBP int64`, `TotalRentalBP int64`, `RateOfSurplusProfitBP int64`). `ComputeDR2Outcome(investments []SuccessiveInvestment) DifferentialRentIIOutcome` pure fn: `TotalRentalBP = ΣSurplusProfitBP`; `AdditionalCapitalBP = ΣCapitalBP`; `RateOfSurplusProfitBP = roundHalfUp(TotalRentalBP×10000, AdditionalCapitalBP)`. `IntensiveExtensiveComparisonID` + `NewIntensiveExtensiveComparisonID()`. `IntensiveExtensiveComparison` (persisted; `IntensiveRentPerAcreBP int64`, `ExtensiveRentPerAcreBP int64`, `TotalCapitalBP int64`, `TotalRentalSameBP int64`).
- **Store.** Interface gains four methods: `CreateDR2Outcome`/`ListDR2Outcomes`, `CreateIntensiveExtensive`/`ListIntensiveExtensive`. `memory.go` adds maps with newest-first sort; `mysql.go` adds the four methods. Migrations `00081_v3_ch41_dr2_constant_schema.sql` (tables `dr2_outcomes` with `productivity_case` column, `intensive_extensive_comparisons`) and `00082_v3_ch41_dr2_constant_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000004100` Case II total £18 rate 36000 bp, `5eed000000000000004101` Case III total £13.50 rate 27000 bp, `5eed000000000000004102` intensive 1800 bp vs extensive 900 bp; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (4).** `POST /v1/rent/dr2-outcomes` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/rent/dr2-outcomes` (`{items:[...]}` never null), `POST /v1/rent/intensive-extensive` (201 + Location), `GET /v1/rent/intensive-extensive` (`{items:[...]}` never null). New api-gateway proxy rules for `/v1/rent/dr2-outcomes` and `/v1/rent/intensive-extensive`.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch41DR2ConstantPrice.tsx` (registered `v3-ch41`, status pending→done): intro on constant regulating price and the three productivity cases; (1) DR&nbsp;II outcomes form — case selector (1–4) + two fixed investment tranche rows (parcel_id, capital_bp, output_quarters, surplus_profit_bp defaults 250/400/900) → `createDR2Outcome`; results table (case label, additional_capital, new_rent/acre, total_rental as £; rate_of_surplus_profit as %); (2) Intensive vs extensive form (intensive_rent_per_acre_bp default 1800, extensive_rent_per_acre_bp default 900, total_capital_bp default 500, total_rental_same_bp default 1800) → `createIntensiveExtensive`; table showing both rent-per-acre columns side by side as £. CSS prefix `v3-ch41`.

### Ch. 40 — Second Form of Differential Rent (Differential Rent II)

`finance-service` extends `internal/rent/` (Vol. III Ch. 40, Part VI) — the **second concrete form of differential rent**, arising from successive capital investments on the **same** parcel (intensive cultivation). DR II presupposes DR I: the baseline surplus-profit is already established by soil-fertility differences; each additional tranche of capital applied to a better soil may yield its own surplus-profit above the regulating price. The lease duration determines who captures newly-formed surplus-profit — the tenant during the lease, the landlord at renewal.

- **Domain types.** `SuccessiveInvestmentID` + `NewSuccessiveInvestmentID()`, `LeaseTermID` + `NewLeaseTermID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `SuccessiveInvestment` (`ParcelID`, `TrancheNumber int`, `CapitalBP int64`, `OutputQuarters int64`, `SurplusProfitBP int64`). `LeaseTerm` (`ParcelID`, `StartYear int`, `DurationYears int`, `FixedRentBP int64`). `DifferentialRentIITable` (computed, not persisted): `TotalCapitalBP = Σ CapitalBP`, `TotalOutputQuarters = Σ OutputQuarters`, `TotalRentBP = Σ SurplusProfitBP`, `RentPerAcreBP = TotalRentBP / acres` (acres ≤ 0 → 1). `ComputeDifferentialRentII(investments, acres)` produces the table. Seed: two £2.50 tranches (CapitalBP=250, SurplusProfitBP=900 each) on parcel `5eed000000000000003700` → TotalRentBP=1800 (£18); one lease term start=1867, duration=21yr, fixed_rent=900 bp.
- **Store.** Interface gains four methods: `CreateSuccessiveInvestment`/`ListSuccessiveInvestments`/`ListSuccessiveInvestmentsByParcel`, `CreateLeaseTerm`/`ListLeaseTerms`. Migrations `00079_v3_ch40_dr2_schema.sql` (tables `successive_investments`, `lease_terms`) and `00080_v3_ch40_dr2_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000004000` tranche 1, `5eed000000000000004001` tranche 2, `5eed000000000000004002` lease term; `-- +goose Down` DELETEs by those IDs).
- **HTTP endpoints (5).** `POST /v1/rent/successive-investments`, `GET /v1/rent/successive-investments` (returns `{items:[...]}`), `GET /v1/rent/dr2-tables/{parcel_id}` (returns `{table, investments}`), `POST /v1/rent/lease-terms`, `GET /v1/rent/lease-terms` (returns `{items:[...]}`). New api-gateway proxy rules for `/v1/rent/successive-investments`, `/v1/rent/dr2-tables`, `/v1/rent/lease-terms`.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch40DifferentialRentII.tsx` (registered `v3-ch40`, status pending→done): DR II intro (intensive vs extensive; lease mechanism); (1) Successive-investment form (parcel_id, tranche_number, capital £, output quarters, surplus_profit bp) → `createSuccessiveInvestment`; after save also fetches `getDR2Table` to show live aggregate; investment list table; (2) DR II aggregate section — "Compute for parcel" input → `getDR2Table`; shows `dl` of total_capital, total_output, total_rent, rent_per_acre (all as £) + tranche breakdown table; (3) Lease-term form (parcel_id, start_year, duration_years, fixed_rent_bp) → `createLeaseTerm`; lease list table. CSS prefix `v3-ch40`.

### Ch. 38 — General Remarks (on differential rent)

`finance-service` extends `internal/rent/` (Vol. III Ch. 38, Part VI) — the **mechanism** of differential rent. A producer commanding a free natural force (waterfall, fertile soil) has a lower individual price of production than the market-regulating general price; the gap is **surplus-profit**, which landed property converts into **differential rent**. The **price of land** is capitalised rent: £10/yr at 5% interest = £200 = 96 000 labour-minutes. No extra value is created — surplus-profit is surplus-value redistributed from capitalist to landowner.

- **Domain types.** `PriceOfProductionSurplusProfit` (persisted; `CapitalID`, `GeneralPriceOfProductionBP int64`, `IndividualPriceOfProductionBP int64`, `SurplusProfitBP int64`; `ComputeSurplusProfit` = general − individual). `MonopolisedNaturalForce` (persisted; `Kind string`, `IsMonopolisable bool`, `ParcelID`). `DifferentialRent` (persisted; `ParcelID`, `SurplusProfitBP int64`, `AnnualRentLabourMinutes int64`). `CapitalisedRentPrice` (persisted; `ParcelID`, `AnnualRentLabourMinutes int64`, `InterestRateBP int64`, `CapitalisedPriceLabourMinutes int64`; `ComputeCapitalisedPrice` = roundHalfUp(rent × 10000, rate_bp)). New ID constructors: `NewPoPSurplusProfitID()`, `NewMonopolisedNaturalForceID()`, `NewDifferentialRentID()`, `NewCapitalisedRentPriceID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). Validation is handler-side (422 on empty ids, negative magnitudes, or non-positive interest rate); `ComputeCapitalisedPrice` guards divide-by-zero by returning 0.
- **Store.** Interface gains eight methods (`CreatePoPSurplusProfit`/`ListPoPSurplusProfits`, `CreateMonopolisedNaturalForce`/`ListMonopolisedNaturalForces`, `CreateDifferentialRent`/`ListDifferentialRents`, `CreateCapitalisedRentPrice`/`ListCapitalisedRentPrices`). `memory.go` adds maps with newest-first sort; `mysql.go` adds the eight methods. Migrations `00075_v3_ch38_differential_rent_schema.sql` (tables `pop_surplus_profits`, `monopolised_natural_forces`, `differential_rents_ch38`, `capitalised_rent_prices`) and `00076_v3_ch38_differential_rent_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000003800`–`3803`: waterfall monopolised natural force on parcel `3700` (`…3800`), differential rent 4800 LM/yr with 2500 bp surplus (`…3801`), capitalised price 96000 LM = £200 at 500 bp / 5% (`…3802`), PoP surplus-profit general 11500 / individual 9000 / surplus 2500 bp (`…3803`)).
- **HTTP endpoints (8).** `POST /v1/rent/surplus-profits`, `GET /v1/rent/surplus-profits`, `POST /v1/rent/monopolised-forces`, `GET /v1/rent/monopolised-forces`, `POST /v1/rent/differential-rents`, `GET /v1/rent/differential-rents`, `POST /v1/rent/capitalised-prices`, `GET /v1/rent/capitalised-prices`. All list endpoints return `{"items":[...]}` (never null). New api-gateway proxy rules for the four new path prefixes under `/v1/rent/`.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch38GeneralRemarks.tsx` (registered `v3-ch38`, status pending→done): waterfall-analogy intro; four create-form + list-table sections — (1) surplus-profit PoP gap, (2) monopolised natural force (kind + is_monopolisable checkbox + parcel_id), (3) differential rent (parcel_id + surplus_profit_bp + annual_rent_labour_minutes), (4) capitalised rent price (parcel_id + annual_rent_labour_minutes + interest_rate_bp; derived `capitalised_price_labour_minutes` shown; note "= roundHalfUp(rent×10000, rate_bp)"). `web/src/types.ts` adds eight interfaces. `api.ts` adds eight `financeApi` functions. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx` (CHAPTER_PANELS + QUOTES).

### Ch. 36 — Pre-Capitalist Relationships

`finance-service` adds `internal/credit/precapitalist_usury.go` (Vol. III Ch. 36, Part V) — the **final chapter of Part V** and the close of the interest-bearing-capital section. **Usury** is the antediluvian form of interest-bearing capital, **older than the capitalist mode of production**: in antiquity and the Middle Ages it bled small producers and the nobility alike at ruinous rates, but it created no new mode of production — it merely impoverished the old one. As industrial capital rises it **subordinates** usury: the usurer becomes a mere department of the credit system, lending to industrial capitalists at a rate **disciplined by the average rate of profit**, so interest can no longer exceed the fraction of profit the productive capitalist can afford to surrender. The invariants encode this history: **wage labour cannot exist in the pre-capitalist stages**, and only the subordinated stage is tied to industrial capital.

- **Domain types.** `UsuryDevelopmentStage int` enum (1=`StageAntiquity`, 2=`StageMediaeval`, 3=`StageTransition`, 4=`StageSubordinated`; `IsValid()` ⇒ 1..4). New sentinels `ErrInvalidUsuryStage`, `ErrWageLabourInPreCapitalist`, `ErrNegativeHistoricalRate`, `ErrMissingSubordination`. `UsurersCapitalID` + `NewUsurersCapitalID()` and `PreCapitalistCreditFormID` + `NewPreCapitalistCreditFormID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `UsurersCapital` (the only persisted entity; `Name`, `Stage`, `WageLabour bool`, `Borrowers []string`, `InterestRateBP int64`, `SubordinatedTo`, `Description`) via `NewUsurersCapital(...)`, which fail-fast validates in declaration order: `!stage.IsValid()` → `ErrInvalidUsuryStage`; `wageLabour && stage ∈ {Antiquity, Mediaeval}` → `ErrWageLabourInPreCapitalist`; `interestRateBP < 0` → `ErrNegativeHistoricalRate`; `stage == StageSubordinated && subordinatedTo != "industrial_capital"` → `ErrMissingSubordination`. Value types: `PreCapitalistCreditForm` (early credit instruments — temple loans, montes pietatis; constructor assigns a fresh ID), `UsurySubordination` (`SubordinatedTo`, `Disciplined bool`, `Description`; compose directly), and `HistoricalInterestRate` (`Period`, `RateBP int64` ≥ 0, `Description`) via `NewHistoricalInterestRate(...)`.
- **Store.** Interface gains three methods (`CreateUsurersCapital`/`GetUsurersCapital`/`ListUsurersCapitals`). `memory.go` adds a `usurersCapitals` map with newest-first sort; `mysql.go` adds the three methods + `scanUsurersCapital`, persisting `Borrowers` as a JSON-encoded `borrowers_json` TEXT column. Migrations `00071_v3_ch36_precapitalist_usury.sql` (table `usurers_capitals`) and `00072_v3_ch36_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000003600`–`3602`: antiquity usury at 4800 bp, mediaeval usury at 2400 bp, subordinated usury at 800 bp under `industrial_capital`).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/credit/usurers-capital` (400 bad JSON; 422 on invariant violations; 201 + Location `/v1/credit/usurers-capital/{id}`), `GET /v1/credit/usurers-capital` (`items` never null), `GET /v1/credit/usurers-capital/{id}` (404 missing). New api-gateway proxy rules for the path prefix plus its `{rest...}` variant.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch36PreCapitalistRelationships.tsx` (registered `v3-ch36`, status pending→done): explainer on usury as the antediluvian form of capital and its subordination under industrial capital; a usurers-capital form (name, development-stage selector, wage-labour toggle, borrowers, interest rate in basis points, subordinated-to, description) enforcing the same invariants as the Go constructor; a live list of stored records fetched on mount and after create. `web/src/types.ts` adds the `UsurersCapital` mirror type and its create input; `api.ts` adds the three client functions. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx`. **COMPLETES Part V (Ch. 21–36)** — interest-bearing capital, credit, and the historical relation of usury to industrial capital.

### Ch. 31 — Money-Capital and Real Capital II

`finance-service` adds `internal/credit/floating_capital.go` continuing Marx's analysis of money-capital and real capital (Vol. III Ch. 31, Part V). Loanable money-capital has **two sources**: (1) the **simple transformation of money into loan capital** — idle post-crisis industrial capital plus the economies in the reserve of means of circulation that banking concentrates — which is **inversely** related to real accumulation (it swells when production stagnates); and (2) the **transformation of capital or revenue into money** that then becomes loan capital, which **accompanies** real accumulation. **Bill-brokers** on **Lombard Street** redistribute the surplus deposits of agricultural districts to the manufacturing centres by **rediscounting** bills. The mass of loanable capital is **not** the quantity of currency: the same **£20 lent five times over performs £100** of loan-capital service.

- **Domain types.** New sentinels `ErrNegativeFloatingAmount`, `ErrInvalidFloatingSource`, `ErrTimesLentTooLow`, `ErrNegativeReserveSaving`. `FloatingCapitalSource` int enum (`SourceSimpleTransformation=1` inverse, `SourceCapitalRevenueConversion=2` accompanies) with `IsValid()`. `LoanCapitalVelocity` value type (`LoanCapitalVelocityID`/`NewLoanCapitalVelocityID()`; `MoneyAmount`, `TimesLent int64` ≥ 1, `LoanCapitalCreated int64` = MoneyAmount × TimesLent) via `NewLoanCapitalVelocity(...)` which **computes** LoanCapitalCreated so the invariant always holds (`ErrTimesLentTooLow` if times < 1). `CirculationReserveSaving` value struct (`Amount int64` pence ≥ 0; `Description`). `BillBroker` value type (`BillBrokerID`/`NewBillBrokerID()`; `RediscountVolume int64`) and `Rediscount` value type (`OriginalDiscounter`, `Broker`, `BillAmount`). `FloatingCapital` (the **only** persisted entity; `FloatingCapitalID`/`NewFloatingCapitalID()` 96-bit crypto/rand hex; `Amount int64` ≥ 0, `Source`, embedded `Velocity`, embedded `ReserveSaving`) via `NewFloatingCapital(...)` (`ErrNegativeFloatingAmount`, `ErrInvalidFloatingSource`, `ErrNegativeReserveSaving`). `RelationToAccumulation()` pure fn names inverse vs. accompanies from the source.
- **Store.** Interface gains three methods (`CreateFloatingCapital`/`GetFloatingCapital`/`ListFloatingCapitals`). `memory.go` adds a `floatingCapitals` map with newest-first sort (createdAt DESC, id ASC tiebreak); `mysql.go` adds the three methods + `scanFloatingCapital`. **Every** field — including all `LoanCapitalVelocity` sub-fields (`velocity_money_amount`, `velocity_times_lent`, `velocity_loan_capital_created`) and both `CirculationReserveSaving` sub-fields (`reserve_saving_amount`, `reserve_saving_description`) — is a column and round-trips identically through Memory and MySQL. Migrations `00061_v3_ch31_floating_capital.sql` (table `floating_capitals`) and `00062_v3_ch31_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000003100`–`3101`: Ipswich farmers' surplus deposits source=2 accompanying accumulation with the £20×5=£100 velocity row; post-crisis idle industrial capital source=1 inverse to accumulation).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/credit/floating-capital` (400 bad JSON; 422 `ErrTimesLentTooLow`/`ErrNegativeFloatingAmount`/`ErrInvalidFloatingSource`/`ErrNegativeReserveSaving`; 201 + Location), `GET /v1/credit/floating-capital` (`items` never null), `GET /v1/credit/floating-capital/{id}` (404 missing). New api-gateway proxy rules for the path prefix plus its `{rest...}` variant.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch31MoneyRealCapitalII.tsx` (registered `v3-ch31`, status pending→done, circuitNode `["M","delta-M"]`): explainer on the two sources of loanable capital and bill-brokers / Lombard Street; a loan-capital velocity calculator (money amount × times lent → loan capital created); two source exemplar cards (Ipswich farmers, post-crisis idle capital); a floating-capital form (amount, source selector, reserve saving) showing the live relation-to-accumulation verdict mirroring the Go pure fn; live list of stored parcels on mount and after create. `web/src/types.ts` adds `LoanCapitalVelocity`, `FloatingCapital`, and `CreateFloatingCapitalInput`; `api.ts` adds the three client functions. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx`.

### Ch. 30 — Money-Capital and Real Capital I

`finance-service` adds `internal/credit/money_real_capital.go` implementing the opening of Marx's question on money-capital and real capital (Vol. III Ch. 30, Part V). The central question: does the accumulation of **money-capital** (loanable funds) correspond to the accumulation of **real capital** (productive capital plus commodities)? Not necessarily — the two can diverge, and in a crisis they diverge violently: loanable money piles up in the banks at the very moment productive capital and saleable stocks contract. **Commercial credit** — the chain of bills of exchange producers draw on one another as a commodity moves down the line of production (spinner → weaver → manufacturer → exporter) — is the **foundation of the credit system** and is tied directly to the rhythm of reproduction. In a crisis, commercial credit contracts not from a shortage of money but because **returns are blocked** (the C→M sale never completes) and mutual confidence collapses. The ultimate cause of real crises remains the restricted consumption of the masses against the limitless drive of production.

- **Domain types.** New sentinels `ErrNegativeReserveCapital`, `ErrNonPositiveBills`, `ErrNegativeReturnDays` (reusing `ErrInvalidCyclePhase` and `IndustrialCyclePhase` from `rate_of_interest.go` — not redeclared). `AccumulationCorrespondence` value struct (`MoneyCapitalGrowthBP`, `RealCapitalGrowthBP int64` bp — each may be positive or negative with **no equality invariant**; `Corresponds bool`; `Explanation`). `CreditLimit` value struct (`ReserveCapital int64` pence ≥ 0; `Description`). `ReturnFlow` value struct via `NewReturnFlow(...)` (`ExpectedDays`/`ActualDays int64` ≥ 0; `IsBlocked()` when actual > expected). `CommercialCreditCircuit` value type only (`CommercialCreditCircuitID`/`NewCommercialCreditCircuitID()`; `Stages []string`; `TotalBillsAmount int64` > 0; `StageCount()`) via `NewCommercialCreditCircuit(...)` (`ErrNonPositiveBills` if ≤ 0). `RealCapitalAccumulation` (the only persisted entity; `RealCapitalAccumulationID`/`NewRealCapitalAccumulationID()` 96-bit crypto/rand hex; `Phase`, embedded `Correspondence`, embedded `CreditLimit`, `CommercialCreditContracts`/`CauseIsMoneyScarcity bool`) via `NewRealCapitalAccumulation(...)` (`ErrInvalidCyclePhase` on bad phase, `ErrNegativeReserveCapital` on negative reserve). `CorrespondenceVerdict()` pure fn names whether the two accumulations align and, when credit contracts, whether the cause is money scarcity or blocked returns + lost confidence.
- **Store.** Interface gains three methods (`CreateRealCapitalAccumulation`/`GetRealCapitalAccumulation`/`ListRealCapitalAccumulations`). `memory.go` adds a `realCapitalAccumulations` map with newest-first sort (createdAt DESC, id ASC tiebreak); `mysql.go` adds the three methods + `scanRealCapitalAccumulation`. Every field — including all `AccumulationCorrespondence` sub-fields (`money_capital_growth_bp`, `real_capital_growth_bp`, `corresponds`, `correspondence_explanation`) and both `CreditLimit` sub-fields (`reserve_capital`, `credit_limit_description`) — is a column and round-trips identically through Memory and MySQL. Migrations `00059_v3_ch30_money_real_capital.sql` (table `real_capital_accumulations`) and `00060_v3_ch30_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000003000`–`3001`: Crisis 1847 with money-capital +8% / real-capital −6% diverging, commercial credit contracting on blocked returns rather than money scarcity; Revival 1850s with the two accumulations corresponding).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/credit/real-capital-accumulation` (400 bad JSON; 422 `ErrInvalidCyclePhase`/`ErrNegativeReserveCapital`; 201 + Location), `GET /v1/credit/real-capital-accumulation` (`items` never null), `GET /v1/credit/real-capital-accumulation/{id}` (404 missing). New api-gateway proxy rules for the path prefix plus its `{rest...}` variant.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch30MoneyRealCapital.tsx` (registered `v3-ch30`, status pending→done, circuitNode `["M","delta-M"]`): explainer on money- vs. real-capital accumulation and commercial credit as the credit system's foundation; a form to record an observation (phase selector, money-/real-capital growth in bp, reserve capital, contracts? / cause-is-money-scarcity? checkboxes) with a live correspondence verdict mirroring the Go pure fn; the Crisis-1847 exemplar card; live list of stored observations on mount and after create. `web/src/types.ts` adds `AccumulationCorrespondence`, `CommercialCreditCircuit`, `RealCapitalAccumulation`, and `CreateRealCapitalAccumulationInput`; `api.ts` adds the three client functions. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx`.

### Ch. 29 — Component Parts of Bank Capital

`finance-service` adds `internal/credit/bank_capital.go` implementing Marx's anatomy of bank capital (Vol. III Ch. 29, Part V). Bank capital divides into two material parts: **cash** (gold coin and bank-notes in the till) and **securities** (commercial paper — bills of exchange — plus public securities such as government bonds, stocks, and mortgages). The greater part is **fictitious**: deposits are mere book entries, and bonds and stocks are titles to a future stream of surplus-value rather than the underlying real capital. Their market value is found by capitalising the income they yield at the prevailing rate of interest — `MarketValue = AnnualIncome × 10000 / InterestRateBP` — so paper values move **inversely** with the rate of interest while the real capital is untouched.

- **Domain types.** `BankCapitalComponentKind int` enum (1=`KindCash`, 2=`KindBillOfExchange`, 3=`KindGovernmentBond`, 4=`KindStock`, 5=`KindMortgage`; `IsValid()`). New sentinels `ErrBankCapitalMismatch`, `ErrInvalidComponentKind`, `ErrNonPositiveInterestRate`, `ErrNegativeDeposit`. `BankCapitalComponent` value struct (`Amount int64` pence, `Kind`, `Description`). `BankCapitalID`/`NewBankCapitalID()` + `FictitiousCapitalValuationID`/`NewFictitiousCapitalValuationID()` + `DepositEntryID`/`NewDepositEntryID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `BankCapital` (persisted; `CashAmount`, `SecuritiesAmount`, `TotalCapital int64` pence, `Components []BankCapitalComponent`) via `NewBankCapital(...)` — computes `TotalCapital = Cash + Securities` so the invariant always holds, validates every component kind (`ErrInvalidComponentKind`). `FictitiousCapitalValuation` (persisted; `NominalValue`, `AnnualIncome`, `InterestRateBP>0`, `DividendRateBP`, `MarketValue int64` pence) via `NewFictitiousCapitalValuation(...)` — derives `MarketValue = roundHalfUp(AnnualIncome*10000, InterestRateBP)` (`ErrNonPositiveInterestRate` if rate ≤ 0). `DepositEntry` value type only (`NewDepositEntry(amount, desc)` enforces `Amount >= 0`; never persisted, as a deposit is a book entry). Reuses `basisPoints`/`roundHalfUp` from sibling files.
- **Store.** Interface gains six methods (`CreateBankCapital`/`GetBankCapital`/`ListBankCapitals`, `CreateFictitiousCapitalValuation`/`GetFictitiousCapitalValuation`/`ListFictitiousCapitalValuations`). `memory.go` adds `bankCapitals` and `fictitiousValuations` maps with newest-first sort (createdAt DESC, id ASC tiebreak), deep-copying the `Components` slice on store/read to match the MySQL JSON round-trip. `mysql.go` adds the six methods + `scanBankCapital` (the `Components` slice persists as a `components_json` MEDIUMTEXT column via json.Marshal/Unmarshal; nil → empty slice on read; component kinds re-validated) and `scanFictitiousCapitalValuation` (all-scalar columns). Migrations `00057_v3_ch29_bank_capital.sql` (tables `bank_capitals`, `fictitious_capital_valuations`) and `00058_v3_ch29_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000002900`–`2901`: Scottish bank £3m cash / £24m securities / £27m total, Bank of England; `5eed000000000000002910`–`2911`: £100 government bond @5% → £100 market value, joint-stock share nominal £100 @10% dividend → £200 at the 5% market rate).
- **HTTP endpoints (6).** `POST /v1/credit/bank-capital` (400 bad JSON; 422 ErrInvalidComponentKind/ErrBankCapitalMismatch; 201+Location), `GET /v1/credit/bank-capital` (`items` never null), `GET /v1/credit/bank-capital/{id}` (404 missing); `POST /v1/credit/fictitious-capital-valuation` (400 bad JSON; 422 ErrNonPositiveInterestRate; 201+Location), `GET /v1/credit/fictitious-capital-valuation` (`items` never null), `GET /v1/credit/fictitious-capital-valuation/{id}` (404 missing). New api-gateway proxy rules for both path prefixes plus their `{rest...}` variants.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch29BankCapital.tsx` (registered `v3-ch29`, status pending→done, circuitNode `["delta-M"]`): explainer on cash vs. securities and fictitious capital; a fictitious-capital calculator (annual income + interest rate → live market value with the inverse-relation table at 2.5/5/10%); a bank-capital form with an editable component list and a computed total; the Scottish-bank exemplar card; live lists of stored bank capitals and valuations on mount and after create. `web/src/types.ts` adds `BankCapitalComponentKind`, `BankCapitalComponent`, `BankCapital`, `FictitiousCapitalValuation`, `DepositEntry`, and the two `Create…Input` types; `api.ts` adds the five client functions. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx`.

### Ch. 28 — Medium of Circulation and Capital

`finance-service` adds `internal/credit/medium_of_circulation.go` implementing Marx's correction of Tooke and Fullarton (Vol. III Ch. 28, Part V). Tooke and Fullarton conflated three distinct categories: money as **medium of circulation** (the *coin function* — consumer/retail revenue expenditure, governed by retail volume and price levels), money as **capital** (the *capital-transfer function* — productive capital moving between capitalists in the circuit M—C…P…C′—M′), and **interest-bearing capital** proper (the M—M′ circuit). Marx shows that only the coin function governs the quantity of notes needed as currency; capital transfers between capitalists require no net addition to the note circulation — they cancel via book entries or bills of exchange. The Bank of England 1844 data illustrates the split: of £20m notes outstanding, the coin function dominates while £8m is held as a reserve against convertibility demands.

- **Domain types.** `CurrencyFunction int` enum (1=`FunctionCoin`, 2=`FunctionCapitalTransfer`; `IsValid() bool`). New sentinels `ErrNonPositiveCurrency`, `ErrFunctionSplitExceedsTotal`, `ErrNegativeReserve`, `ErrInvalidCurrencyFunction`. `ReserveFund` value type (`Amount int64 >= 0` in pence; `Description`). `CurrencyObservationID` + `NewCurrencyObservationID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `CurrencyObservation` (persisted entity; `TotalCurrencyOutstanding`, `CoinFunctionAmount`, `CapitalTransferAmount int64` in pence; `ReserveFund`) via `NewCurrencyObservation(name, total, coinAmount, capitalTransferAmount, reserveFund, description)` — invariants `total > 0` (`ErrNonPositiveCurrency`), `coin + capitalTransfer <= total` (`ErrFunctionSplitExceedsTotal`), `reserve >= 0` (`ErrNegativeReserve`). Pure helpers: `CoinShareBP()` (coin function as basis-point share of total, division-by-zero-guarded), `ClassifyCurrency(obs)` (predominantly-coin ≥7500bp / mixed ≥2500bp / predominantly-capital-transfer), and `NewTookeFullartonAnalysis()` returning the three conflated categories plus Marx's correction. Supporting value objects `TookeFullartonAnalysis`, `RevenueExpenditureCircuit`, `CapitalTransferCircuit`.
- **Store.** Interface gains three methods (`CreateCurrencyObservation`/`GetCurrencyObservation`/`ListCurrencyObservations`). `memory.go` adds a `currencyObservations` map with newest-first sort (createdAt DESC, id ASC tiebreak). `mysql.go` adds the three methods + `scanCurrencyObservation` (flattens `ReserveFund` into `reserve_amount`, `reserve_description` columns). Migrations `00055_v3_ch28_medium_of_circulation.sql` (table `currency_observations`) and `00056_v3_ch28_seed.sql` (seed IDs `5eed000000000000002800`–`2801`: Bank of England 1844 £20m outstanding / £14m coin / £4m capital-transfer / £8m reserve; Scottish Banking System high-credit-velocity with capital-transfer dominant and minimal metallic reserve).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/credit/currency-observations` (400 bad JSON; 422 on ErrNonPositiveCurrency/ErrFunctionSplitExceedsTotal/ErrNegativeReserve; 201+Location), `GET /v1/credit/currency-observations` (`items` never null), `GET /v1/credit/currency-observations/{id}` (404 missing). New api-gateway proxy rules `/v1/credit/currency-observations` and `/v1/credit/currency-observations/{rest...}`.
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch28MediumOfCirculation.tsx` (registered `v3-ch28`, status pending→done, circuitNode `["M","delta-M"]`): Tooke/Fullarton three-category conflation explainer with Marx's correction; Bank of England 1844 and Scottish-velocity exemplars; create form for a currency observation (validates coin+capital-transfer ≤ total client-side) with live coin-share preview; stored-observations list with classification badge. `web/src/types.ts` adds `CurrencyFunction`, `ReserveFund`, `CurrencyObservation`; `api.ts` adds the three client functions. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx`.

### Vol. III Ch. 20 — Historical Facts about Merchant's Capital

`finance-service` adds `internal/merchant/historical_merchant.go` as a sibling to `money_dealing.go`. **This chapter completes Part IV.** Merchant's capital is historically the oldest free form of capital, predating the capitalist mode of production: in pre-capitalist trading systems it profited by *unequal exchange* between under-developed producing communities, not by the exploitation of wage-labour. As industrial capital develops, merchant's capital is progressively subordinated to it — the more developed industrial capitalism, the higher the share of merchant capital that functions as a mere agent of productive capital (the inverse-development law).

- **Domain types.** `DevelopmentStage int` (const 1–3: `StagePreCapitalist`/`StageTransition`/`StageSubordinated`; `IsValid() bool`). New sentinels `ErrInvalidDevelopmentStage`, `ErrSubordinationOutOfRange`, `ErrWageLabourInPreCapitalist`. `HistoricalMerchantCapitalID` + `NewHistoricalMerchantCapitalID()` (96-bit crypto/rand hex). `HistoricalMerchantCapital` (persisted entity) via `NewHistoricalMerchantCapital(name string, stage DevelopmentStage, profitSource string, wageLabour bool, subordinationIndex int64)` — `ErrInvalidDevelopmentStage` if stage invalid, `ErrSubordinationOutOfRange` if `subordinationIndex ∉ [0, 10000]`, `ErrWageLabourInPreCapitalist` if a pre-capitalist form claims wage-labour (invariant 1). `SubordinationIndex` is basis points (merchant capital ÷ total social capital × 10000). `InverseDevelopmentRelation` + `StagePoint` value objects (verify `SubordinationIndex` rises monotonically as the stage advances — invariant 2). `HistoricalTradingSystem` (named exemplar) and `PreCapitalistForm` (forces `ExploitsWageLabour == false`) value objects. Reuses `basisPoints`/`roundHalfUp` from sibling files.
- **Store.** Interface gains three methods (`CreateHistoricalMerchantCapital`, `GetHistoricalMerchantCapital`, `ListHistoricalMerchantCapitals`). `memory.go` adds `historicalMerchantCapitals map`. `mysql.go` adds three methods + `scanHistoricalMerchantCapital` (all-scalar columns; `stage` TINYINT → `DevelopmentStage`, `wage_labour` TINYINT(1) → bool). Migrations `00039_v3_ch20_historical_merchant.sql` (table `historical_merchant_capitals`) and `00040_v3_ch20_seed.sql` (four seed records `5eed000000000000002001`–`5eed000000000000002004`: Venice carrying-trade & Genoa republic at StagePreCapitalist with rising SubordinationIndex 500/800, Dutch carrying-trade at StageTransition 3000, industrial subordination at StageSubordinated 7000 — monotonic by stage).
- **HTTP endpoints (3).** `POST /v1/merchant/historical-forms` (400 bad JSON; 422 ErrInvalidDevelopmentStage/ErrSubordinationOutOfRange/ErrWageLabourInPreCapitalist; 201+Location), `GET /v1/merchant/historical-forms` (list, `items` never null), `GET /v1/merchant/historical-forms/{id}` (404 missing). Proxied through api-gateway (`/v1/merchant/historical-forms`, `/v1/merchant/historical-forms/{rest...}`).
- **React panel.** `web/src/chapters/vol3/Ch20HistoricalMerchantCapital.tsx` (registered `v3-ch20`, status flipped pending→done): historical timeline of the named trading systems ordered by development stage with their rising subordination index, illustrating the inverse-development law; create-form (name, stage, profit source, wage-labour, subordination index). `web/src/types.ts` adds `DevelopmentStage`, `HistoricalMerchantCapitalResponse`, `HistoricalTradingSystemResponse`; `api.ts` adds `createHistoricalMerchantCapital`, `listHistoricalMerchantCapitals`, `getHistoricalMerchantCapital`. Wired into `ChapterShell.tsx`.
